### First build of a new TypeScript app (3 October 2026)

- Measured on `swhurl-try-6` (before): the first build took 145 s (37 s from job start to first step, `Build the image` 49 s against 9 to 14 s warm); warm builds took 54 to 73 s. The cause of the 37 s start was not found and did not recur.
- Template `v0.1.3` (PR #22; catalogue pin `7971009`) makes every build read a shared registry cache, `ghcr.io/samclement/swhurl-app-template-typescript-cache:<kind>-<database>`, written by the template's own runs on `main` (public, since a package pushed from a public repository's workflow is). An app no longer writes its own Actions cache: exporting every layer took 29 s of a 37 s first build. A missing cache is a warning, and writing it needs a GHCR login before the build.
- Measured on the throwaway `swhurl-try-7` with an empty Actions cache: 71 s end to end (`Build the image` 16 s, smoke test 12 s, push 5 s), every dependency layer `CACHED`. Not measured: the whole console flow after this change, Kotlin (no cache for it yet), and what a changed `package-lock.json` costs (dependency layers miss until the template's next push to `main`).
- Template `v0.1.4` (PR #25; catalogue pin `63b807f`) replaces the smoke test's fixed 10 s wait (twice for SQLite) with a 4 s poll that fails the moment the container stops. Template CI, smoke-test step: 5 s for web and worker without a database (11 to 21 s before), 9 to 11 s with SQLite. The poll was checked locally against a container that keeps running (passes after 4 s) and one that exits after 2 s (fails at 2 s). A failure that appears later than 4 s after a healthy first response (a worker's first tick fails after the 60 s default interval) is no longer caught here.
- End to end, terminal path, TypeScript web app without a database (`swhurl-try-8`, template `v0.1.4`), seconds from `make app-repo`: repo created and first image published 60 (one run, no queue wait); `make app-new` done 64; pushed 72 (manual steps); Flux unit created 81; HelmRelease Ready 96; Certificate Ready and `https://staging-swhurl-try-8.homelab.swhurl.com/` answering 302 (sign-in) 115. Against 2 to 2.5 minutes reported before, mostly the first build (145 s). Not included: the console's own job overhead and the add-to-staging PR's `validate` check (about 32 s before auto-merge, from earlier PRs). Left running for the operator to remove (`make app-remove`, then delete the repository and package).
- Kotlin template `v0.1.3` (PR #12; catalogue pin `e2546da`) gets the same shared registry cache (`ghcr.io/samclement/swhurl-app-template-kotlin-cache:<kind>-<database>`, public, all four tags written by the template's run on `main`). Throwaway `swhurl-try-9` (web, no database, empty Actions cache): first build 91 s end to end (`Build the image` 51 s, smoke test 13 s, push 6 s), against 6 min 31 s through the console earlier (the first Gradle build). The dependency layer is pulled from the registry (about 277 MB) and `check shadowJar` still runs for every app, since the application name is in the source. Not measured: Kotlin through the console end to end, or a SQLite or worker combination cold. The Kotlin smoke test was not shortened (only TypeScript has the 4 s poll). Left for the operator to delete: repositories `swhurl-try-7` and `swhurl-try-9`, the live instance `swhurl-try-8/staging` with its repository, and the ghcr packages `swhurl-try-*`, `swhurl-visibility-test-b` and `-c1`.

- Offline Stage 1 follow-up (5 October 2026): the candidate patch now targets `samclement/hello-ts` base `5c2abb6b47158f2409e6bcf8c7fe94f579984b5e`; `check_patch_applies` passed against a clean clone at that exact SHA. Codex CLI final-message parsing rejects nonzero exit, empty output, malformed JSON, non-object JSON and oversized output; `diagnosis.schema.json` defines the structured result. The query policy exposes ClickHouse `max_result_bytes` and `max_result_rows` settings, and its streaming reader rejects responses above 64 KB before returning any data. No incident-review live integration was added or exercised.

Plan section 14 was rewritten for it.

Plan section 4 holds the task list (stage A, then B); section 14 was amended and starts after it.

## Team platform T0: call identity and network policy proofs (10 October 2026)

Two throwaway live tests for [plan section 15](plan.md#15-team-platform--delivered-10-to-11-october-2026), run at `12a86b5`. Neither changes an existing namespace; both left nothing behind (no test namespaces afterwards).

- `make live-test-network-policy` passed (8 checks). Before the policy a pod in another namespace reached the app and Traefik served `https://network-policy-test.homelab.swhurl.com` on its default certificate. With a policy selecting only the app's pods and admitting the Traefik pods on 8080: the other namespace was blocked and stayed blocked after 20 seconds, Traefik still returned 200, the app stayed Ready, and an unselected pod on 8089 in the same namespace stayed reachable. Deleting the policy let the other namespace in again. k3s enforces NetworkPolicies on app pods.
- `make live-test-call-identity` passed (8 checks, about 10 minutes). A callee with no API token accepted a caller's 600-second service-account token for audience `callee`, checking the signature against the cluster's public keys mounted as a ConfigMap; the token named `system:serviceaccount:call-identity-caller:caller`; the Kubernetes API answered 401 to it; a token for another audience and a request without a token were refused; the kubelet replaced the token file before expiry and the new token was accepted.
- First attempt, failed and changed: the test bound `system:service-account-issuer-discovery` to `system:unauthenticated` so the callee could fetch `/openid/v1/jwks` itself. The API server answered 401 with and without the binding: it refuses anonymous requests. The test's temporary ClusterRoleBinding was removed by its cleanup, and the design now hands the public keys to each app as a file (plan T2.1).
- Not exercised: a real HTTP-01 certificate issuance under a policy (an unselected pod on 8089 stood in for the solver); a same-namespace caller; rotation of the cluster's signing key.

## Team platform T2: calls and discovery (11 October 2026)

[Plan section 15](plan.md#15-team-platform--delivered-10-to-11-october-2026), task T2, verified at `a2867b7`. `make check` passed before each commit (497 tests; `make check-apps` passes for 13 instances). Both template repositories' Template workflows passed on the commits pinned in `STACK_REVISIONS` (`ea294aa` TypeScript, `e7dd18e` Kotlin; four combinations each).

- **Existing apps** (`19f6ac3`): all seven restarted once with `SWHURL_APP`, `SWHURL_ENV`, `SWHURL_AUTH_ISSUER` and `SWHURL_AUTH_KEYS`, and came back 1/1 Ready. `make verify-platform` passed, with the new "Call identity" section: `apps/signing-keys.json` holds the cluster's one signing key and the issuer matches.
- **Three throwaway apps**, each created with `make app-repo` and `make app-new`: `swhurl-try-10` (TypeScript, SQLite, team payments), `swhurl-try-11` (TypeScript, team insights, created with `--calls swhurl-try-10`) and `swhurl-try-12` (Kotlin, team insights). `make app-calls` then added try-12 → try-10 and try-11 → try-12.
- **A declared call works** (entered at the caller's example route from the Traefik pod, as a routed request arrives): try-11 → try-10 returned 200 with the callee reporting `caller: {type: app, id: swhurl-try-11}`. Across stacks: try-12 (Kotlin) → try-10 (TypeScript) and try-11 (TypeScript) → try-12 (Kotlin) both returned 200 with the right caller.
- **Undeclared and bad callers are refused at both layers.** Network: a pod of `hello-ts-staging` could not connect to try-10. App (requests sent from the Traefik pod, which the network admits, with real tokens minted by `kubectl create token` and never printed): a malformed token, a token for another audience, and a correctly addressed token from an app not on the allowed list each got 401 from try-10; the Kotlin app answered 401 to a malformed token and to a token from an app it does not allow. A correct token got 200; a request with no token got 200 as `anonymous`. An undeclared call from try-12 to try-11 failed in the caller: it has no address for it.
- **One trace, two apps, two teams** (ClickHouse): trace `879be7a3…` has try-11's server and client spans (`swhurl.team = insights`) and try-10's server span (`payments`, `caller.type = app`, `caller.id = swhurl-try-11`); both apps' log lines carry the trace and the caller. try-10 logged each refusal with its reason (`malformed token`, `token is for another app`, `hello-ts is not allowed to call swhurl-try-10`). The Kotlin caller's trace `78fa9057…` likewise spans try-12 and try-10.
- **One field name for both stacks** (`a2867b7`): logback writes the caller inside `mdc`; after the collector change, lines from all three apps carry `caller.type` and `caller.id`.
- **A certificate issued under a NetworkPolicy:** `swhurl-try-10-tls` became Ready about 30 seconds after the instance was created (ACME order `valid`), the real case T0 and T1 left unexercised.
- **Backup under a quota:** `make backup-sqlite` backed up try-10's database (its helper pod runs in the quota-limited namespace); `make verify-platform` then passed.
- Not exercised: the console's **Calls** form and a console-created app with `calls:` in its `swhurl.yaml` (unit-tested; needs a signed-in browser); a first promotion of an app with calls on the live cluster (unit-tested); `make app-remove` refused for a called app on the live tree (unit-tested); `make platform-signing-keys` after a real key change; the kubelet's token replacement inside a real app over more than ten minutes (proven for a plain pod in T0). `swhurl-try-12` runs the Kotlin template one commit before its log-message fix, so its "request handled" lines show `{}` placeholders.

## Team platform T4: events (11 October 2026)

[Plan section 15](plan.md#15-team-platform--delivered-10-to-11-october-2026), task T4, verified at `cf1ccc4`. `make check` passed before each commit (506 tests; `make check-apps` passes for 14 instances). Both template repositories' Template workflows passed on the pinned commits (`086161f` TypeScript, `81f7d8b` Kotlin).

- **The broker** (`1e714ac`): `platform-nats` became Ready in under a minute: `nats-0` 2/2 with JetStream (1 GB store on a 2Gi volume) and `nats-controller` 1/1. The controller signed in with its nkey, which also proves the pure-Python key encoding against a real server.
- **Declared through Git:** `make app-events` gave `swhurl-try-10` `--publish visited` and a new worker, `swhurl-try-13` (team payments), `--subscribe swhurl-try-10.visited`. After the push the controller created stream `staging-swhurl-try-10` and consumer `swhurl-try-13_visited` (both Ready), and NATS ran with three users.
- **Publish and receive:** a request to try-10 published `swhurl-try-10.visited` and try-13 logged `event received` with the same event id and the publisher's trace id. An event published while try-13 was down was delivered when it came back.
- **One trace through the broker** (ClickHouse): trace `4c514e0a…` has try-10's server span, its producer span `publish swhurl-try-10.visited` and try-13's consumer span `process swhurl-try-10.visited`.
- **Across stacks:** after `swhurl-try-12` (Kotlin) was updated from the template and given `--publish visited`, a request to it produced trace `c32536a2…`: its server and producer spans (team insights) and try-13's consumer span (team payments).
- **Permissions hold.** From inside try-13's pod with its own key, NATS refused: publishing try-10's event, publishing a production subject, reading a consumer it did not declare, and the JetStream API. Reading its own consumer was allowed. `make app-events` refused a subscription to an event nobody publishes.
- **Start-up race, found and fixed:** try-13 first started seconds before NATS had loaded its user, was refused ("Authorization Violation") and, with the first helper, never tried again. The helpers now retry every five seconds. Reproduced on purpose by removing and re-adding the subscription (`3247dad`, `418a502`): twelve refused tries over about a minute, then delivery. The delay is the kubelet refreshing NATS's mounted configuration.
- **Health and logs:** `make verify-platform` passed with its Events section (NATS running, controller running, declared streams and consumers exist). Logs from `nats-0` and the controller are in ClickStack under namespace `messaging`. `make check-secrets` passed for the four new Secrets.
- Not exercised: a Kotlin app subscribing on the live cluster (unit-tested and compiled; only Kotlin publishing was run live); events in production, and that staging cannot read production (no production instance declares events; the permission is by subject name, as tested for the `prod.` publish); a redelivery after a handler throws; a stream reaching its size or age limit; the controller restoring a stream deleted by hand; replacing a key; a NATS restart or data loss; an upgrade of either chart.

At `6409ea0` (console image `src-f74903fd63b68621`, pinned by the publish workflow in `9f398fb`; steps in `d995913`, `57ac0a5`, `2db6790`, `1c75879`, `6409ea0`): Validate and Publish console image passed; `make flux-reconcile` and `make verify-platform` passed and the console Deployment rolled out on that digest. Before the pushes, a local `console --dev` against the live cluster was driven in headless Chromium (Playwright): on Platform, **Suspend** on `platform-nats` opened the dialog naming the unit and Cancel left the row unchanged; **Reconcile** on the same row answered in the row ("reconcile started · job #1") without leaving `/platform`, the page read itself again 10 seconds later with the units section still open, and the audit log recorded the job as succeeded; on Teams, opening Delete closed Edit (one form at a time). Apps showed 8 apps in about 330 px of rows (840 px before), and while a push was reconciling showed the Updating rows with their reason and no button. Apps and Teams were read at 500 px.

Found while reviewing and fixed in `2db6790`: Platform once listed a unit as failing ("Reconciliation in progress") beside a units summary saying all healthy, because the checks and the units list read the cluster a moment apart; what needs attention now comes from the units read alone (tested).

**Teams redesigned the same day** (`27d4383`, console image `src-36d9e5e2df992789`, pinned in `5f5602d`), after the operator found the rows cramped and the actions unclear: one card per team with its apps as a table (language, staging, prod), one **Edit** instead of Edit and Rename. Validate and Publish console image passed; `make flux-reconcile` and `make verify-platform` passed. Through a port-forward to the deployed console, `/teams` returned 200 with three cards (platform, payments, insights), eight language pills read from the apps' own repositories (seven TypeScript, one Kotlin) and three Edit forms; the first read took 1.4 s and the next 0.26 s (languages kept). In headless Chromium against a local `console --dev`: Edit on `payments` showed the description and the name filled in with `payments`; opening Delete closed Edit and the buttons did not move.

**Rows that open** (`7bce735`, console image `src-c1fa38f454acfb0d`, pinned in `f75df6c`): Apps, the Flux units on Platform and the jobs on Activity became lists whose rows open. Validate and Publish console image passed; `make flux-reconcile` and `make verify-platform` passed. Through a port-forward to the deployed console, `/apps` returned 200 with 8 rows that open, language pills (seven TypeScript, one Kotlin) and promotion labels (two staging ahead, one same image, five staging only); `/platform` returned 27 unit rows that open, 25 with **Reconcile now** (not the two root units); `/activity` and `/teams` returned 200; signed out, https redirected to Google sign-in. In headless Chromium against a local `console --dev` on the live cluster: an Apps row opened to both environments and stayed open when the page was re-read, with no horizontal scroll at 1280 px; on Platform, **Suspend** in the opened `platform-nats` row asked first and Cancel changed nothing, **Reconcile now** answered there ("reconcile started · job #1"), the row was still open after the page re-read itself and its buttons were back; on Activity that job opened to its output ("applied revision main@sha1:a0bb0c94621b…"). The in-row **Reconcile** on Apps and Platform superseded the per-row buttons recorded above.

**Promotion labels** (`05a1955`, console image `src-fb9f21c571bfa4b9`, pinned in `d9b30b1`): Validate, Publish console image, `make flux-reconcile` and `make verify-platform` passed; the deployed `/apps` showed two **Prod behind** (amber), one **In sync** (green) and five **Staging only** (grey), replacing the labels counted above. **Images differ** had no live case (tested offline).

**Dashboard links** (`6445fc8`, console image `src-625591e0005e3979`, pinned in `d6ad64c`): the dashboard job now records each app's HyperDX dashboard id in the `console/app-dashboards` ConfigMap and the console links it. Validate, Publish console image, `make flux-reconcile` and `make verify-platform` passed. Within a minute of the rollout the job logged "recorded 8 dashboard addresses for the console" and the ConfigMap held eight app-to-id entries (field managers `kustomize-controller` and `dashboard-sync`); a further `make flux-reconcile` left all eight in place. `kubectl auth can-i` as `dashboard-sync`: get and patch on `app-dashboards` yes; get `notification-state`, list ConfigMaps and get Secrets no. As `console`: get `app-dashboards` yes; patch it, get `notification-state`, list ConfigMaps and get Secrets no. From inside the HyperDX pod, `/dashboards/<hello-ts id>` returned 200 on the `dashboards/[dashboardId]` route and `/dashboardz/<id>` 404. Through a port-forward to the deployed console, `/apps` and `/teams` each carried eight distinct dashboard links and `/apps/hello-ts/staging` one. Not exercised: opening a link in a signed-in browser, and a new app's link appearing after its first deploy.

**App page** (`7c867fe`, console image `src-58e32e74b46868c2`, pinned in `2181f97`): the app page became a summary and one card per part (Use, Connect, Operate), sharing the card with Teams and the jobs list with Activity. Validate, Publish console image, `make flux-reconcile` and `make verify-platform` passed. Through a port-forward to the deployed console, `/apps/hello-ts/staging`, `/apps/hello-ts/prod` and `/apps/swhurl-try-13/staging` (a worker) returned 200 with the summary, five cards and four change forms each; `/teams` and `/activity` still returned 200. In headless Chromium against a local `console --dev` on the live cluster, on `/apps/test-3/staging`: opening **Scale** closed **Change calls** (one form at a time); **Reconcile** answered in the Operate card ("reconcile started · job #1") without leaving the page, and after the page re-read itself the job was listed under Jobs and opened to its output ("applied revision main@sha1:dfcb7e813deb…"); no horizontal scroll at 420 px. Not exercised: submitting a change form from a card (the forms post the same fields as before, tested), and a failing or suspended app's page on the live cluster.

Not exercised: the pull-request table and **Hold** / **Request auto-merge** on Activity against real open pull requests (rendered from made-up records; `merge_mode` is unit-tested), **Reconcile** and **Resume** in an opened Apps row (the same request as on Platform, which was exercised), a row opening by itself for a failing environment on the live cluster (tested offline), submitting a team form from the cards (the forms post the same fields as before, tested), and everything in a real signed-in browser session.

- Live delivery (`f50942e`): `make channel-add NAME=payments-try` read a random topic from standard input (never printed), `make team-edit NAME=payments ARGS="--notify payments-try"` routed the team, and a memory-request change on `swhurl-try-13` (team payments) caused a Helm upgrade. The checker logged `Notification delivered: swhurl-try-13/staging deployed` and then the same with `(channel payments-try)`; the message was read back from the throwaway topic at 16:08:04 UTC with its title, normal priority and the app-page link. `make verify-platform` reported one team route matching Git and one channel Secret.

- Console forms: a local preview on made-up data showed the channel list, the team's ticked channels, and **New channel** with **Generate** filling the address and showing its subscribe link (headless Chromium screenshots). Offline tests cover that an address reaches only the command's standard input and is absent from job output, the pull request, the commit message and both pages.
- Deployed console (`a17773f`, image `src-3c2dd2497f8d9492` pinned in `2364712`): Validate and Publish passed, `make verify-platform` reported the image current and no channel, and through a port-forward `/teams` showed the Notification channels card with **New channel**, its Generate button and "No channels yet"; signed out, the public address answered 302.
- Not exercised: the channel forms and a team's channel ticks as real pull requests from the deployed console, in a signed-in browser; a channel that cannot be reached (retry, then given up after two hours); a second channel on one team; an unhealthy, recovery or rollback message on a channel (the same code path as `deployed`).

## Notify service, N2 (11 October 2026)

Commits `3bff453` (code), `a155a62` (manifests, the checker's token and URL) and `fa451ec` (fix) deployed the [notify service](services.md#notify-service); the publish run pinned `src-92a6070e1456125d` in both the console's and the service's HelmRelease (`777b35e`).

- `make check` passed (565 tests); Validate and Publish console image passed for each commit. `platform-notify` is Ready with one `notify` pod.
- Found live and fixed: every token was answered 503, because `kubectl create` validates on the client by listing CRDs, which the service's ServiceAccount may not do. `fa451ec` sends the TokenReview with `--validate=false`; the other reads and the outbox write were tried from inside the pod and worked.
- Who may send, through a port-forward with tokens made by `kubectl create token` (never printed): no token, a forged one, the checker's token for the audience `orders` and its API token each got 401; an app's token (`swhurl-try-13`) and the console's, both for the audience `notify`, got 403 and were logged as refused; the checker's token for `notify` got 202 with no channels; an unknown field got 400. From the console pod, the service's address did not answer (NetworkPolicy); only the checker's pods are admitted.
- Delivery (`0a33125`): with a throwaway channel on team payments, a memory change on `swhurl-try-13` produced `swhurl-try-13/staging deployed` on the platform topic, the checker logged its copy as delivered to the notify service, the service logged it accepted for one channel and delivered to `payments-try`, and the message was read back from the throwaway topic at 16:52:05 UTC with its priority and app-page link.
- When copies were first switched on, the checker also offered the service its copies of two `swhurl-try-13` deployments from the previous two hours (the platform topic had them already); no channel existed then, so nothing was delivered late. A team with a channel at that moment would have received them.
- `make verify-platform`: the notify service runs the console's image; the channel checks passed. The throwaway channel, its route and the memory change were then removed again.
- Not exercised: the notify service being down (the checker's retries, `notify service unreachable` and its recovery are unit-tested; inducing it would page the operator), a pod restart with entries in the outbox, a channel that cannot be reached, and a full outbox.

### One channel per team (11 October 2026)

Operator decision: a team has one notification channel. `make team-edit --notify` accepts one or none and `make check-apps` (rule `notify-channels`) refuses a second (`b9054b4`). The console then dropped its list of named channels: each team card has one **Channel** action (set, new address, remove) backed by `make team-channel`, and the New app form marks each team that has a channel. `make check` passed; the reworked card was seen in a local preview on made-up data (headless Chromium), with Generate filling the address. Offline only: no team has a channel at present, and the forms have not been used in a signed-in browser.

- Limits: with the app's own token, naming another app or `prod` got 403. Of 31 further calls, 29 were sent and 2 were not: the service answered 429 after 30 in the hour, the helper returned false and logged why, and the topic held exactly 30 of the app's messages.

- Not exercised: the Kotlin helper on the cluster (its tests and build passed), a worker app sending, and the reworked Teams page forms in a signed-in browser.

## Default app alerts, N4 (11 October 2026)

Commit `f1a7278` gave every app two [default alerts](apps.md#alerts), kept by the dashboard job, and the notify service a `POST /v1/alert` for ClickStack's webhook; the publish run pinned `src-5884d1b541cab5c6` (`72186c1`).

- Spike first, with a throwaway alert and webhook that were deleted again: the HyperDX API created and deleted an alert on a dashboard tile; a webhook's `headers` were not stored; a fired alert sent its name as the title and a body with a `Group:` line naming the namespace, the value and the time range.
- `make check` passed (578 tests). After `make flux-reconcile`, `make clickstack-notify-webhook` created the `swhurl notify` webhook (the secret was never printed), and the next run of the dashboard job created 18 alerts, two for each of the nine apps; the run after reported `9 app(s) have their default alerts` and wrote nothing.
- Fired and recovered: with a throwaway channel on team payments, twelve failed requests to `swhurl-try-14` (`/call/nope`, HTTP 502) produced `swhurl-try-14/staging alert: failed requests or runs` on the team's topic at 18:10:02 UTC, high priority, with `12 meets or exceeds 5`, the time range and a link to the dashboard; `swhurl-try-14/staging recovered: failed requests or runs` followed at 18:15:01 with normal priority. The service logged the alert as accepted from ClickStack for one channel and delivered.
- The throwaway channel was removed afterwards; no team has a channel now, so the 18 alerts deliver to nobody until one is set.
- Not exercised: the `error-logs` preset firing (the same path), a wrong webhook secret from ClickStack itself (unit-tested; the live endpoint was reached only by the real webhook), an alert for a production instance, and alerts being removed with their app.

## Alert follow-ups and the removal of swhurl-try-14 (11 October 2026)

- `swhurl-try-14/staging` was removed with `make app-remove` (`165c4c5`): its namespace was gone within four minutes, and the next run of the dashboard job deleted its dashboard; its two alerts went with it (16 left for eight apps). Its GitHub repository and package remain for the operator to delete.
- Commit `36bb91a` (console image `src-5ba673358030a183`, pinned in `e1a9b7d`): `make app-alerts` and `alerts:` in `swhurl.yaml`, a `verify-platform` warning for each team that owns apps and has no channel, and a dashboard job that fails while the `swhurl notify` webhook is missing. `make check` passed (584 tests); Validate and Publish passed.
- Per-app settings, live: `make app-alerts APP=swhurl-try-13 ARGS="--error-logs 20 --errors none"` wrote the annotation; after the reconcile the job logged `replaced swhurl:swhurl-try-13:error-logs` and `deleted swhurl:swhurl-try-13:errors`, and HyperDX held that alert with threshold 20 (15 alerts in all). `--default` then put it back.
- `make verify-platform` named the teams that own apps and have no channel as warnings and reported the dashboard and alert sync as succeeding.
- Not exercised: the job failing for a missing webhook on the live cluster (unit-tested; deleting the webhook would stop every app's alerts), `alerts:` from a real app repository's `swhurl.yaml`, and the setting surviving a real promotion.
- Alert tags (`9144085`, console image pinned by the publish run): each default alert now carries its app's name as a tag beside `swhurl-app`, and a differing alert is updated in place. A throwaway alert first showed that the HyperDX API stores tags on create and on update. After the reconcile the dashboard job updated the existing alerts, all 16 read back with `['swhurl-app', '<app>']`, and the following run wrote nothing. Not exercised: filtering by the tag in the HyperDX alerts page in a browser.

## Promotion merge gate runs two checks (11 October 2026)

Commit `d7782fe` (console image `src-e188ff509a5edf6b`, pinned in `ccbc8db`) made the [auto-merge gate](console.md#auto-merge) check a promotion's merge result with `make check-repo` and `make check-apps` only; a new app still gets all of `make check`.

- Why: promoting `test-3` to production at 19:45:17 UTC (`8f23bf8`) reached `main` at 19:46:20 and its `test-3/prod deployed` message at 19:48:05. The gate's merge step took 41 s of that, nearly all of it `make check`; Flux fetched the push in 2 s and Helm finished 21 s later.
- The checker's 19:47:06 pass sent nothing because an unrelated push to `main` (19:46:55) had the `app-test-3-prod` unit waiting on its dependencies between 19:46:59 and 19:47:10, and a "deployed" message needs the unit Ready; the next pass sent it.
- On this host the two checks take 10 s against 89 s for `make check` (585 tests passed). Validate and the publish run passed on `d7782fe`; after `make flux-reconcile`, `make verify-platform` passed with the new console image running.
- Not exercised: a real promotion through the narrowed gate (no staging build was waiting), so the saving on GitHub's runner, estimated at 30 s, is not measured.
- Checker follow-up (`130fefa`, console image `src-a09317bf38d4da41`, pinned in `56a32cf`): a "deployed" message no longer waits for the app's Flux unit, and a held one logs why ([expectations](services.md#notification-expectations)). `make check` passed (587 tests); Validate and the publish run passed; after `make flux-reconcile`, `make verify-platform` passed and the checker's jobs complete on the new image. Not exercised: a deployment finishing while another push to `main` is being applied (unit-tested).
- First real run of both changes: the first promotion of `swhurl-try-10` (PR #53, commit `06ab5c1` at 20:48:02 UTC) reached `main` at 20:49:33, Helm finished the install at 20:50:00 and `swhurl-try-10/prod deployed` was delivered at 20:50:05, 123 s after the commit (168 s for `test-3` earlier). The gate's merge step took 17 s (41 s before). The pull request ran the whole Validate workflow (44 s, against 19 s for an image-only promotion) because a first promotion registers its unit in `clusters/home/kustomization.yaml`; the Auto-merge run started by the label gave up at 20:48:25 ("Validate has not passed") and the one started by Validate's completion merged it. Still not exercised: a later, image-only promotion through the narrowed gate.
- Validate's fast path now covers the unit list ([validation](contributing.md)): a new app or a first promotion also changes `clusters/home/kustomization.yaml`, which used to send its pull request through the whole workflow. In a scratch worktree, a list entry for a missing unit file failed `make check-repo` only, and removing `platform.yaml` from the list passed `check-repo`, `check-apps` and all 587 tests: the jobs the fast path skips never read the list. Not exercised: a new app or first promotion on the fast path (expected Validate time 19 s, from 44 s).
- The Auto-merge run that the label starts now waits for Validate (`265f053`, console image `src-9c8ff365c3479fd2`, pinned in `f084c15`): every 5 s for up to two minutes, where it used to ask once and leave the merge to a second run. `make check` passed (590 tests), Validate and the publish run passed and the new console image is running. Not exercised: a console pull request merged by a run that waited.
- All four changes together: the first promotion of `test-4` (PR #54, commit `22e8c26` at 21:18:41 UTC) reached `main` at 21:19:18, Helm finished the install at 21:19:40 and `test-4/prod deployed` was delivered at 21:20:06, 85 s after the commit (123 s for `swhurl-try-10`, 168 s for `test-3`). Validate took the fast path (15 s; tooling, template and collector jobs skipped), the Auto-merge run started by the label did the merge (its step ran 21:19:04 to 21:19:18, starting as Validate's last job ended) and the second run had nothing to do (2 s step). The merge step prints nothing while it waits, so whether it waited is not shown. Still not exercised: an image-only promotion, and a new app.

## What an app offers: intake (12 October 2026)

Stage 3 of [plan section 17](plan.md#17-connections-and-contracts--designed-12-october-2026), commit `7e4b9d5` (console image `src-8a8270ed1e39074d`, pinned in `4cb6258`). How it works: [what an app offers](apps.md#what-an-app-offers). Template releases `v0.2.1` (TypeScript `f6733259e330106cbadb34e3f37cfed9c0a62698`, Kotlin `aaba44f91a67ccb3c3451675bae2ecabc7c2500e`) no longer list the example event in `swhurl.yaml`.

- `make check` passed (599 tests); Validate and the publish run passed; `make verify-platform` passed with the console on the new image.
- **NATS permission:** after the reconcile the server's configuration holds `staging.swhurl-try-10.>` and `staging.swhurl-try-12.>`. A request to `swhurl-try-10` (port-forward) published `swhurl-try-10.visited`, and `swhurl-try-13`'s log shows that event's id within five seconds.
- **`make app-offers ARGS="--all staging"`** against GitHub read all eight staging apps at their images' short commits; none names documents yet, so each "offers nothing (unchanged)". The Offers workflow (run by hand, `38220656216`) printed the same and committed nothing.
- **Found while releasing:** a TypeScript Template job on `main` hung for 30 minutes. Every app imported `ajv` and `yaml` at start-up, the worker test allowed one second for three runs, and on failure it left its child running. Fixed in template PR #32 (lazy import, a ten-second wait, the worker always stopped, a 20-minute job limit in the TypeScript `app.yml`); the Kotlin `app.yml` has no job limit.
- Not exercised: an app that names documents (none exists): creation with the features, the workflow after a real image commit, a new event stored before its document is read, a refused document, a promotion carrying offers through the auto-merge gate, and the app page's **Describes itself** line. Each is unit-tested.

## What an app offers, proved with swhurl-try-15, and the catalogue (12 October 2026)

Stages 3 and 4 of [plan section 17](plan.md#17-connections-and-contracts--designed-12-october-2026). The instance passed the policy and runs 1/1.

Flux committed the new tag (`c0398d7`, 09:46:23 UTC); the Offers workflow committed `df7adbb` 21 seconds later (the kept `asyncapi.yaml`, `source.yaml`, the release's annotation and `SWHURL_EVENTS_PUBLISH: visited,greeted`), with nobody involved. A request to the new pod logged no "event not published".

Seen in a local `console --dev` at 1280 pixels with two rows open.

Merged after its checks passed (`f270e32`) and applied: `swhurl-try-13` was refused by NATS once (`CONNECTION_REFUSED`, the expected wait for its new permission), then logged two `swhurl-try-15.greeted` events: the one published before it subscribed and the one after.

## Existing apps merged; two faults found (12 October 2026)

The operator merged four of the five stage 5 pull requests (`swhurl-try-10`, `-11`, `-13`, `hello-ts`); `swhurl-try-12` #4 is open. Each image deployed to staging by itself.

- **Fault: the Offers workflow failed five times** (11:08 to 11:14 UTC). `SWHURL_API_PATH`, set when offers are read (`9ef063a`), was not among the settings environments may differ in, so `env-drift` failed the policy for `hello-ts` and `swhurl-try-10`, which have production instances, and nothing was committed. The earlier proof used `swhurl-try-15`, which has none. Fixed in `92e1b01`; the next run committed `b615ca4` (offers for `hello-ts`, `swhurl-try-10` and `swhurl-try-11`). `make check-apps` passes for 17 instances and the live catalogue shows "8 APIs · 4 events · 7 described by their app", including `hello-ts`'s `/repeat`.
- **Fault: a promotion of `hello-ts` was refused** at 11:14:28 UTC with "staging must be healthy and running the reviewed digest". Staging was healthy: Flux's commit for `swhurl-try-12` had landed about four seconds earlier, so the latest commit on `main` was not yet applied to `hello-ts`. The refusal stands; it now says that Flux is still applying the latest commit and to try again (`92e1b01`, console image `src-947d4a352ffac475`).
- A trial `make app-promote APP=hello-ts` in the checkout (reverted, nothing committed) changed `apps/hello-ts/prod/helmrelease.yaml`, added `apps/hello-ts/prod/offers/` and passed the policy. Not exercised: that promotion made from the console and merged.

## Demo apps (12 October 2026)

Step 2 of [plan section 18](plan.md#18-demo-team-and-apps--decided-12-october-2026). Four public repositories, made with `make app-repo` and all in team `demo`: `samclement/demo-orders`, `demo-shop`, `demo-shopper` (TypeScript) and `demo-notifier` (Kotlin). Platform commits `8978bdb`, `34abb24`, `b086699`.

- **Running:** each is 1/1 in staging on its own code; `demo-orders` is also in production (`b086699`, retained volume bound, certificate Ready), where `/` answers and `POST /orders` from a person answers 401.
- **Traffic:** `demo-shopper` visits every half minute. In one minute the shop's log shows three `POST /buy` (201) and three `GET /` from the app `demo-shopper`, and demo-orders' shows three `POST /orders` (201) and three `GET /orders` from the app `demo-shop`.
- **Events:** each order is published as `demo-orders.created` and `demo-notifier` logs it; none failed to publish. `demo-orders`' published list went from the template's `visited` to `created` through the Offers workflow, with nobody involved.
- **Telemetry in ClickStack, last ten minutes:** spans, logs and metrics for all four apps, every span carrying team `demo`; `demo-orders`' server spans name the caller (`app:demo-shop`); one trace runs `demo-shopper → demo-shop → demo-orders → demo-notifier`. A dashboard is recorded for each app.
- **Notification:** a purchase of 12 biscuits through the shop as a signed-in person (port-forward with the sign-in header) made `demo-notifier` log "team told about a big order: 12 biscuits, placed through demo-shop. (accepted: true)". That sent one real message to the `platform` channel. The steady traffic buys one to three and notifies nobody.
- **Catalogue:** for team `demo`, "2 APIs · 1 events · 3 described by their app", each with its repository's description.
- **Backups:** `make verify-platform` reported no SQLite backup for the two new `demo-orders` databases; `make backup-sqlite` made and uploaded one for each, and the check passes.

Faults found and fixed on the way:

- **The first Kotlin subscriber received nothing.** The Java NATS client reads the stream's description before binding to its consumer and was refused ("Permissions Violation for Publish to $JS.API.STREAM.INFO.staging-demo-orders"). A subscriber may now read the description of the streams it reads (`b46a563`); the refusals stopped once NATS loaded the list.
- **A first promotion of an app that describes itself was refused** ("the release is annotated … but the instance keeps no offers/"): production was made from staging's release, annotation included, before it had the documents (`b086699`).
- `app-promote` printed "Set production secrets" for an app whose only Secret is its events key (`b086699`).

Not exercised: the shop in a signed-in browser; the notification arriving on a phone (the service accepted it); `demo-shop`, `demo-shopper` and `demo-notifier` in production; a first promotion of a described app through the console and its merge gate.

## Console jobs across a restart (12 October 2026)

`9713d2a`, console image `src-6605e9ecf1f5e6fa` (deployed as `597be21`): a console pod being replaced finishes its running jobs first, jobs are remembered in `console/console-jobs`, and an unfinished new app can be finished from its job page ([console](console.md#jobs-and-restarts)). The console's pod has `terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 900`; `make verify-platform` passed.

Exercised with reconciles of `platform-reloader`, started through a port-forward as the operator:

- **Recorded:** job 1 succeeded and its record (6 lines, the pod's name as owner) was in the ConfigMap.
- **Finished first:** job 2 was started and its pod deleted at once. The record said `running` a second later; the pod went away about 6 seconds after, with the job `succeeded` and written by that pod. The pod that replaced it listed jobs 1 and 2 on Activity and served `/jobs/2`.
- **Interrupted:** job 3 was started and its pod killed with `--grace-period=0 --force`. The next pod marked it `interrupted`, kept its 3 lines of output and said so on its page; job 4, on the same unit, then ran normally and continued the numbering.
- **Not exercised:** **Finish creating this app** on a real interrupted new-app job (it would create a real repository; covered by `make test`), a job long enough to hold a pod for minutes, and a job page left open in a browser across the restart.

  | Apps/Services | 7 | 0.98 s | 0.41 to 0.48 s |

- **Same pull requests as before:** run offline with the old builders (`6e6136f`) and the new values over 25 forms (scale, access, connect, disconnect, remove, team add, edit and remove, channel set and remove, new apps of both stacks), the commands, standard input, titles, descriptions, branch names, job names, resume records and merge rule were identical.
- **Through the real app, stopping before the commit:** the console's own handlers with a dry-run runner, GitHub read only and `main`'s tooling in a downloaded clone. A CPU change to `demo-orders/staging` and a new description for team `demo` each ended `Dry run: would commit ... no PR opened`; the commands' own rules refused the rest (2 replicas on a single-writer app, publishing an event its offers do not describe, removing an app `demo-shop` calls); a malformed value answered 400 and an unknown change 404.
- **A fault that run found** (`ad89a28`): outside the image, each command left `__pycache__` files in the clone, and every file a change leaves there is committed, so a pull request opened from `make console-dev` would have carried about 30 bytecode files. The image sets `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE`; the command now sets it itself, and a test with a real interpreter fails without it.
- **Offline:** `make check` passed (639 tests).
- **Not exercised:** a real pull request opened by the deployed image through each form (it needs a signed-in browser), and finishing a new-app job from a record written by the previous image (the record's shape is unchanged and tested).

## One backup set for MongoDB and SQLite (12 October 2026)

`c3502f0` puts what a backup set is in one module ([`backupset.py`](../tools/swhurl/backupset.py)): the stamp, the archive and metadata file names, which archive is newest, its age, retention, the checksum and the S3 upload. `recovery.py`, `sqlite_backup.py` and `sqlite_restore.py` only fill and read archives, `retention.py` is gone and `make verify-platform` judges both kinds with one rule. A refactor: nothing on the cluster changed. Console image `src-cd65157ef8b9ecde` (deployed as `1639ccf`); Validate passed, and after `make flux-reconcile`, `make verify-platform` passed with no warning and `make app-status APP=demo-orders ENV=staging` says Healthy.

- **Real backups, without pruning** (`PRUNE=false`): `make backup-mongodb` wrote `clickstack-mongodb-20261012T215420Z` and uploaded its two files (18 already in the bucket); `make backup-sqlite` wrote `sqlite-20261012T215424Z` for the four app databases (`demo-orders` and `swhurl-try-10`, staging and prod) and uploaded two files each. The metadata files hold the same keys in the same order as that morning's.
- **Restores:** `make live-test-restore-mongodb` restored that archive into `recovery-test` (collection counts and the team ingestion key matched) and `make live-test-restore-sqlite` passed (one, two restored; the replaced two, three kept beside it; HelmRelease resumed and Ready); both deleted their namespaces. `make restore-sqlite APP=demo-orders ENV=staging DRY_RUN=true` chose `sqlite-20261012T215424Z.db.age` with its checksum checked.
- **A fault fixed:** the MongoDB restore test picked the newest archive by file time, which after `aws s3 cp` is the order of copying. With that morning's and that evening's backups copied to a scratch `BACKUP_DIR`, the older one last, the dry run chose the evening one.
- **Checks:** `make verify-platform` reported the newest MongoDB backup and each of the four SQLite backups, on the host and in S3, as 0.0 to 0.1 h old. `prune-backups --dry-run` on the real directory would prune only that morning's MongoDB backup (two on one day) and keep 9.
- **Offline:** `make check` passed (648 tests); the dry-run variants CI runs printed their plans.
- **Not exercised:** the 03:30 timer running the new code with pruning on (first run 13 October), a real `make restore-sqlite` on an app instance, and a failed upload or a cut-short stream outside the unit tests.

## One Notice and one ntfy sender (12 October 2026)

`1dc8c3a` makes a message for a person one value ([`notice.py`](../tools/swhurl/notifications/notice.py)): `Notice` holds the title, message, priority and link and writes itself for ntfy, for an outbox entry and for the notify service; the checker, the notify service and the host heartbeat send through one sender, and the platform topic has one reader with one address rule. A refactor: what is sent and what an outbox stores did not change. Console image `src-b1bcfe7d3e4be21a` (`eb34a98`), which the checker and the notify service run too.

- **Same messages as before:** run offline with the previous commit's tooling and the new, the checker's outbox entries, the notify service's entries for a request, an app's own message and a firing and a recovered ClickStack alert, both HyperDX webhook definitions, and every HTTP request (the heartbeat's and the checker's to ntfy, the checker's copy to the notify service with its token header) were identical.
- **The checker, live:** the first `console-notifications` job on the new image posted `console deployed` to the platform topic (ntfy answered 200) and ended with nothing pending.
- **The notify service, live:** a purchase of 12 tea through `demo-shop/staging` (port-forward with the sign-in header) made `demo-notifier` send `demo-notifier/staging: Big order`; the new pod accepted it for one channel and delivered it to channel `platform` a second later (ntfy answered 200). One real message.
- **ClickStack's webhooks:** `DRY_RUN=true make clickstack-ntfy-webhook` and `clickstack-notify-webhook` each reported the live webhook up to date, so neither body changed.
- **A fault that run found:** those two commands run with the system interpreter, which has no `httpx`, and the first draft imported it with the platform topic's reader. It is now imported only where a notice is sent, and a test loads the command without it.
- **Host heartbeat:** the timer's 22:20 UTC run used the new code and succeeded (checker fresh, nothing to send); `make notifications-check` previewed 13 instances with nothing pending.
- **Checks:** `make check` passed (659 tests); `Validate` passed on `1dc8c3a`; `make verify-platform` passed with no warnings (notification channels, notify service on the console's image, checker and heartbeat).
- **Changed on purpose:** the ClickStack webhook command accepts the platform topic on any HTTPS ntfy server, as every other sender does; it used to refuse anything but `ntfy.sh`. The live topic is on `ntfy.sh`.
- **Not exercised:** a real stale or recovered message from the host heartbeat, the checker's copy of an app message reaching the notify service (no app was deployed or failed in this window), and a ClickStack alert arriving at `/v1/alert` from HyperDX itself.

## Each area owns its live checks (12 October 2026)

`4ed543c` moves each live check beside what it checks (`flux.py`, `clickstack.py`, [`apps/checks.py`](../tools/swhurl/apps/checks.py), [`notifications/checks.py`](../tools/swhurl/notifications/checks.py), [`console/checks.py`](../tools/swhurl/console/checks.py), `dashboards.py` and the backup modules) and leaves [`verify.py`](../tools/swhurl/verify.py) as the list of them (145 lines, from 835). A refactor: what `make verify-platform` checks and prints did not change. Console image `src-74fa58e2a48725ee` (`87eb773`).

- **Same output as before:** on the live cluster, the previous commit's `verify-platform` (from an export of `ba408e4`) and the new one printed the same 147 lines but one: the export is not a Git checkout, so it could not compare the console image with itself.
- **After the deploy:** `make verify-platform` passed with 99 `[OK]` lines and no warnings, with the notify service and the console on the new image. The console's Platform page, read through a port-forward with the sign-in header, showed its checks from the new image (Ingress, Image Automation with 9 passing, Notify service, App dashboards and alerts, Notifications) and no error; a `console-dashboards` job on the new image succeeded.
- **With the system interpreter:** `make check-config` (now in `validate.py`) and `make verify-platform` both run without `uv`; a test loads both modules with `httpx` and `ruamel` unavailable.
- **Offline:** `make check` passed (661 tests); `Validate` passed on `4ed543c`. The table of checks in [operations](operations.md#check-health) is printed from the registry and a test compares the two.
- **A stall this deploy met, not caused by the change:** the `console-notifications` CronJob started no job from 22:44 to 22:49 UTC. Helm updated it as its 22:44 job was created; the k3s controller logged `error syncing CronJobController console/console-notifications ... the server could not find the requested resource` four times and did not queue it again (`console-dashboards`, updated in the same second, carried on). Adding and removing an annotation on the CronJob made the controller look again: job `29864089` ran on the new image within ten seconds, delivered `console deployed`, and the jobs after it ran each minute. The gap was under the heartbeat's ten minutes, so no stale alert was sent. The symptom and fix are in [troubleshooting](operations.md#troubleshooting).
- **Not exercised:** a failing check of each kind on the live cluster (the unit tests cover them), the `live-test-team-scenario` test whose ClickHouse reads moved to `clickstack.clickhouse`, and whether that CronJob stall recurs on a later console deploy.

Stages of [plan section 19](plan.md#19-conditional-readability--approved-13-october-2026) preserve the decisions and outputs while giving comparison, validation and transition responsibilities their own names.

- **Stage 1, log comparator (`668d780`):** 15 log tests passed, including ordered failures, missing-field defaults, attribute subsets, preserved fields and original-line checks with no fixture contents in diagnostics. Scheduled notification job `console-notifications-29864556` succeeded on this image.
- **Stage 2, small expressions (`a39cd7d`):** all six production chained ternaries from the starting scan are gone. Exact file-status output remains covered; webhook output now has six state/dry-run combinations. 177 targeted tests and `make check` (678 tests) passed. Image `src-bb4c98555d3ff1ee` (`157e53f`) deployed; reconcile and platform verification passed, `demo-orders/staging` remained Healthy at the applied revision, and checker job `console-notifications-29864563` succeeded on the image.
- **Stage 3c, heartbeat (`1f21006`):** freshness observation and alert/recovery/reminder decisions are separate functions. All 13 heartbeat tests and `make check` (680 tests) passed, including time boundaries, invalid timestamps and input immutability. Image `src-2f36a5d7c7dd2140` (`5d44a44`) deployed; reconcile and platform verification passed, `demo-orders/staging` was Healthy, and checker job `console-notifications-29864570` succeeded on the image. The host heartbeat completed successfully at 07:45:53 BST, after the commit at 07:45:26 BST.
- **Stage 3a, app policy (`55dbaed`):** the checker now calls named pod/container, single-writer and ingress checks in its existing pass order. 39 targeted tests and `make check` (681 tests) passed; the new ordered multi-violation test passed before and after extraction. The policy also imported with `ruamel` explicitly blocked. Image `src-4346961b507ee94d` (`9a1ed0a`) deployed; reconciliation and platform verification passed and `demo-orders/staging` was Healthy at the applied revision.
- **Stage 3b, declaration parsing (`79e7e94`):** `apps/declaration.py` now names resource, event, provides and call default parsing while keeping the shared field validator and validation order. The public tests protect source-prefixed first errors, empty/falsy defaults and bool/int distinctions. 12 targeted tests and `make check` (683 tests) passed. Image `src-1197a18a39cd659b` (`897a449`) deployed; reconciliation and platform verification passed, `demo-orders/staging` was Healthy at the applied revision, and `console-notifications-29864630` succeeded on the new image.
- **Stage 3d, console PR progress (`ae82854`):** `console_prs` delegates one pull request's progress and checks link to `pull_progress`; 64 targeted tests and `make check` (688 tests) passed, preserving request order, status precedence, fallback wording and listing behavior. Image `src-99607cfa4225eef3` (`aa9e977`) deployed; reconciliation and platform verification passed, `demo-orders/staging` was Healthy at the applied revision, and `console-notifications-29864637` succeeded on the new image. Signed-out requests returned HTTP→HTTPS 301 and HTTPS→Google sign-in 302. A real signed-in Activity-page check is pending.
- **Stage 4, exposure edits (`1befeb9`):** `expose` now delegates route validation, host resolution, route mutation and dependency mutation. 36 targeted tests and `make check` (689 tests) passed; old/new results matched for 144 fixture cases including file bytes, and all 112 refusal cases left every file unchanged. Image `src-afd1916b09a75dc7` (`413b8dc`) deployed; reconciliation and platform verification passed, `demo-orders/staging` was Healthy at the applied revision, and `console-notifications-29864645` succeeded on the new image.
- **Stage 5a, notification observation (`9f93732`):** `evaluate` delegates active-instance observation and retired-instance pruning. 36 targeted tests and `make check` (693 tests) passed; 128 complete state/outbox old/new comparisons matched. Image `src-cc7fc1634d1bd620` (`6bba756`) deployed; reconciliation and platform verification passed, `demo-orders/staging` was Healthy at the applied revision, and `console-notifications-29864652` succeeded on the new image.
- **Stage 5b, notification events (`9858b56`):** lifecycle events now carry eligibility, notice text, state updates and inferred-removal handling through one named sequence. 39 targeted tests and `make check` (696 tests) passed; 1,440 complete state/outbox old/new comparisons matched across repeated passes and lifecycle boundaries. Image `src-f072c36d840dfc58` (`bb51ae5`) deployed; reconciliation passed, `demo-orders/staging` was Healthy at the applied revision, and `console-notifications-29864659` succeeded on the image. ## A commit elsewhere does not hold a promotion (13 October 2026)

`8e09e7f`, console image `src-fd3b804091dcf99e` (deployed as `a309603`): the promotion gate asks which files changed between the commit staging's unit applied and main's newest, and holds a promotion only for a change to staging's own folder or unit file ([apps](apps.md#a-commit-elsewhere-does-not-hold-a-promotion)); the review is no longer pinned to a revision. `make check` passed (703 tests); after `make flux-reconcile`, `make verify-platform` passed with the new image running.

- **At rest:** through a port-forward, the review pages of `demo-orders`, `swhurl-try-13` and `test-1` answered 200 with every unit applied at `a309603`.
- **A commit elsewhere, live:** `0ef872f` (this page only) was pushed at 11:45:56 UTC while the review pages of `demo-orders`, `demo-notifier` and `swhurl-try-13` were requested in a loop. The console's log shows seven `GET /compare/a309603...0ef872f` between 11:46:03 and 11:46:14, so their units were behind main for those requests, and all seven review pages answered in that time (11:46:08 to 11:46:18) were 200; each would have been a 409 before.
- **Fault, caused by the test:** a second run started a review request every half second for 45 seconds without waiting for the answers (about 90 at once, each downloading the repository from GitHub). `/healthz` stopped answering within its timeout, the kubelet restarted the console's container at about 11:47:30 UTC and it was Ready again at 11:48:25; no job was running and none is recorded as interrupted. **Open:** a burst of review pages can starve the console's health check and restart it.
- **Not exercised:** a promotion refused live for an unapplied change to staging's own files, and `make app-promote` asking a checkout's history while staging is behind (both covered by `make test`); a comparison of 300 files or more.

In its pod the console answers each `kubectl get ... -o json` it can translate with one request to the API server as its service account ([how](contributing.md#operator-tooling)); writes, logs and Secrets still run `kubectl`.

- **Before** (image `src-cd562c0eb032f5b6`, measured inside the pod): one read took 50 to 120 ms through `kubectl` and 4 to 16 ms as a request; the overview's cluster reads took 1067 ms in 16 `kubectl` processes and an app page's 1132 ms in 13. Listing the console's pull requests from GitHub, which the overview does beside them, took about 380 ms with none open.

- **Deployed:** console image `src-b1d8a10413b52c6b` was pinned by `fe0c802` and `make flux-reconcile` applied it. Inside the new pod, the same reads through both runners (median of 5, `kubectl` then the API): overview reads 1173 and 186 ms, apps list 592 and 130 ms, platform checks 930 and 82 ms, an app page 958 and 232 ms, with no process started for any of them over the API. Both runners returned the same 15 app rows, units and platform checks, and the same `NotFound` and `Forbidden` wording and exit code for an absent unit and a ConfigMap the account may not read. Signed out, `http://` redirected to `https://` and every page to Google; both checker CronJobs completed a job on the new image. `make verify-platform` failed once straight after the rollout (the failing line was not captured) and passed on the next two runs.
- **Not exercised:** a signed-in browser session (page times as a browser sees them, with rendering and the sign-in check, were not measured), and a reconcile, suspend or resume started from the console on this image: their waits now poll over the API.

## Activity settles changes superseded by a later merge (13 October 2026)

`2809219`: only the newest merged pull request for an app instance uses its current cluster state. An earlier merged create or update row ends at **Merged to main** when a later change has already superseded it, instead of appearing to roll out again.

- **What prompted it:** PR #62 added `test-5/staging`; later PR #64 removed it. Activity read the current absent state for both merged PRs, so the older add row incorrectly showed **In progress** at **Flux applies it — not created yet**.
- **Offline:** `make check` passed (727 tests); GitHub `Validate` passed on `2809219`.
- **Deployed:** the publish workflow pinned console image `src-04510fd4ee68b44c` in `cad907f`. `make flux-reconcile` reached every unit Ready at `cad907f`; `make verify-platform` passed; `make app-status APP=demo-orders ENV=staging` reported Healthy, desired/applied revisions matching and 1/1 ready.
- **Activity route:** through a port-forward to the deployed console with an identity header, `/activity` returned 200. PR #62's row showed **Merged to main** and PR #64's row showed **Removed from staging**. Signed out, HTTP redirected to HTTPS and HTTPS redirected to Google sign-in.
- **Not exercised:** an interactive signed-in browser session; the activity for a second update superseded by a newer update (the same latest-merge comparison handles it).

## Activity edge cases settled (13 October 2026)

`cd9a75d`, from a review of the stages: a job's own page settles a superseded change as Activity does; a stage whose start was cut with a record's output keeps its lines; how a pull request ended is read from GitHub once and kept, so a GitHub failure no longer turns finished rows to **Unknown**; a closed new-app pull request says its repository and image still exist.

- **Offline:** `make check` passed; GitHub `Validate` passed on `cd9a75d`.
- **Deployed:** the publish run pinned console image `src-60c33066dd95803e` in `df53bdf`; `make flux-reconcile` applied it, the console rolled out on it, `make verify-platform` passed, and a `console-notifications` and a `console-dashboards` job ran on the new image.
- **Live, through a port-forward to the console's Service with the identity header set by hand:** job 8's page (the `test-5/staging` add that PR #64 later removed) ends at **Merged to main**, where it said Flux was still creating the instance; job 11's page ends at **Removed from staging**. `/activity` took 3.1 s on its first read in the new pod (six merged pull requests read from GitHub) and 0.7 s on the next, with no pull request read again. Signed out, `http://` redirected to `https://` and that to Google sign-in.
- **Not exercised:** a signed-in browser session; a GitHub failure on the live console (tested offline); a record cut to 20 kB (the largest live one is 3.5 kB; tested offline); a closed-unmerged new-app pull request.

## A merged change is judged against its own commit (13 October 2026)

`7352f6e`: a row's **Flux applies it** and terminal stop come from whether the revision Flux has applied contains the merge commit, and whether a later commit to the instance's own files is applied too ([stages of a change](console.md#stages-of-a-change)); an ending is kept with the job's record and not judged again. A failed change that still needs someone stays out of **Finished**. This replaces the day-long read of the instance's state and the latest-merge comparison of `2809219`.

- **Offline:** `make check` passed (734 tests); GitHub `Validate` passed on `7352f6e`.
- **Deployed:** the publish run pinned console image `src-fe2b5ad2572a0704` in `a611b7d`; `make flux-reconcile` applied it, the console rolled out on it, `make verify-platform` passed, and a `console-notifications` and a `console-dashboards` job ran on the new image.
- **Live, through a port-forward to the console's Service with the identity header set by hand:** the first `/activity` judged the six merged changes in the records and took 8.6 s (a pull request read, a comparison and a record write each, one after another); the next took 0.4 s and asked GitHub about none of them. The three promotions ended at **Live in prod**, both removals at **Removed from staging**, and the `test-5/staging` add that PR #64 later removed at **Merged to main** ("a later change to test-5/staging has been applied since"). `console/console-jobs` holds each ending (`live`, `removed`, `superseded`) on records written by earlier pods. Job pages 8 and 10 returned 200.
- **Not exercised:** a signed-in browser session; a change followed live from its merge (the merge not applied yet, then the ending found); an instance failing after its merge, and its row staying in the first list; an open pull request with failed checks; GitHub unreadable for a comparison. All are tested offline.

## TypeScript compilation once (13 October 2026)

TypeScript template PR [#36](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-app-template-typescript/pull/36), released as immutable tag `v0.2.2` at `0c0d3c7`, compiles/type-checks in Docker once, runs tests against that JavaScript, and copies it through the required check stage into `checked-runtime`. The generated Container workflow opts in together with the Dockerfile; older callers retain their runner checks when upgrading only the shared workflow. Tests and development dependencies stay out of the runtime image.

- **Offline and CI:** `make check` passed before the template and catalogue commits (734 tests, schemas, app policy, all 32 rendered template combinations, OTel fixtures and lint); host/fixture shell syntax and every workflow bash step passed. All seven TypeScript image builds and smoke tests passed locally and in GitHub Template runs [38471302551](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-app-template-typescript/actions/runs/38471302551) and [38471550477](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-app-template-typescript/actions/runs/38471550477), including SQLite migration/write/restart persistence. Deliberately invalid types, a failing test and an older Dockerfile with the new target each stopped image creation. The runtime contains compiled JavaScript, with no test directory or TypeScript compiler.
- **Existing app:** reviewed Copier update [demo-shop PR #3](https://github.com/samclement/demo-shop/pull/3), merged at `71e0154`, adopts the Dockerfile and workflow input while preserving its custom code and dependency lock. Its 16 tests and read-only image smoke test passed locally and in GitHub. The main run's log contains one compiler execution and one 16-test success summary; the later image push reused cached compilation and tests, and no separate runner type-check/build script ran.
- **Sample timings:** demo-shop's main workflow [38472209797](https://github.com/samclement/demo-shop/actions/runs/38472209797) took 54 s from creation to completion, against 66 s for [38250787149](https://github.com/samclement/demo-shop/actions/runs/38250787149). Its PR check [38472020151](https://github.com/samclement/demo-shop/actions/runs/38472020151) took 58 s against 72 s for [38250124574](https://github.com/samclement/demo-shop/actions/runs/38250124574). These are individual observations, with runner/cache variation; they do not establish an average speedup or include the subsequent GitOps rollout.
- **Catalogue and console:** platform commit `e8eec77` pins the release and documents adoption. Validate [38472196916](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-platform/actions/runs/38472196916) and publish [38472368469](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-platform/actions/runs/38472368469) passed; bot commit `e3b2fe1` deployed console image `src-0cc2251d3b1d27d8`. Successful notification-checker and dashboard jobs ran on this image after the rollout.
- **Image delivery:** GHCR accepted `demo-shop:8-71e0154` at 21:39:58 UTC, but GitHub's webhook deliveries had no new package event and Flux's last scan was 20:56:34. `make app-hooks` with the existing age key explicitly selected reported every webhook in place. A GitHub test ping returned 200 at 21:46:11; the scan at 21:46:12 selected the new digest, image automation committed `a1a77e8`, and Offers updated the source in `9d3c6dc`. No Flux-owned manifest was applied by hand. The missing publication event is a follow-up in [plan section 0](plan.md#0-where-this-paused-and-what-is-left); recovery and the hourly fallback are documented with the [image webhook](services.md#image-webhook).
- **Live:** `make flux-reconcile` applied `9d3c6dc`; `make verify-platform` passed. `make app-status APP=demo-shop ENV=staging` reported Healthy, 1/1 Ready, desired image `8-71e0154` at digest `e583fdecf515…` running, and TLS Ready. Both staging-demo-shop and console return HTTP 301 and signed-out HTTPS 302. Through Service port-forwards with the identity header supplied directly, the app's `/`, `/healthz` and `/openapi.json` returned 200, `/` returned `latestOrders`, and the console's app page returned 200 and showed `8-71e0154`.
- **Not exercised:** real Google-signed-in browser requests, repeated timing benchmarks, a brand-new repository created from this release, or adoption by the remaining TypeScript apps. Production promotion was not performed.

## Tested PR image reuse (13 to 14 October 2026)

Delivered with helper commits `5b917c3` and `3c1be32`, catalogue commit `53d4ac7`, TypeScript template `v0.2.4` (`f1a7ac6`, template PR #38) and Kotlin template `v0.2.3` (`0d8997d`, template PR #25). Both templates passed their seven GitHub combinations on PR and main; the default combination exports an attested OCI image, loads its runtime for a smoke test, and proves the config matches the retained export. All 32 rendered platform-policy combinations, `make check` (747 tests), Ruff, ShellCheck and all host/fixture `bash -n` checks passed.

The 13 helper tests cover equal source trees across commit identities, changed source/base inputs, expiry, failed and fork runs, unsupported Dockerfiles, corrupt archives and forged proof, runtime loading/config mismatch and publication refusal on altered archives, wrong destinations or changed published digests. A real local OCI copy preserved the manifest digest, and a real daemon-transport load preserved its runtime config. The template export check stopped the initial unsupported attestation-plus-Docker-load build before smoke testing; the corrected flow exports OCI once and loads only its runtime, keeping the original archive and provenance.

### Exact image publication

| App | Reviewed Copier adoption | Successful PR → main runs | Published staging image |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| demo-shop | PR #9, merged `394096f` | [PR 38480786001](https://github.com/samclement/demo-shop/actions/runs/38480786001) → [main 38480980997](https://github.com/samclement/demo-shop/actions/runs/38480980997) | `ghcr.io/samclement/demo-shop:21-394096f@sha256:cee493c856a252dbed66e403a1ac799815922257956c13faa4a4abcc25ecba35` |
| demo-notifier | PR #5, merged `75c4a0c` | [PR 38480795998](https://github.com/samclement/demo-notifier/actions/runs/38480795998) → [main 38481060698](https://github.com/samclement/demo-notifier/actions/runs/38481060698) | `ghcr.io/samclement/demo-notifier:9-75c4a0c@sha256:ff1387f59d4d759381fcfa64083c1f9e55a35e9f54a8f235748689389d938e68` |

Each main log reports `matching tested PR image` and publishes the exact OCI root digest from its PR's proof. Main skips Buildx setup, build and smoke testing. The downloaded OCI indexes contain SLSA provenance v1 whose subject matches their runtime manifest; copying the complete root digest preserves that provenance. The app source trees match across PR checkout and squash merge (`a768fdf9779b6c117bbed67e8020ddf897a0e2c3` and `4c4802407fd31c2de5dbe93d145411105dea29c1`). Copier changed only the caller and saved template version on these final PRs; concurrent Renovate dependency changes stayed in their own commits.

A real mismatch also passed: a dependency update landed between demo-shop PR #6's check and merge. [Main run 38478903878](https://github.com/samclement/demo-shop/actions/runs/38478903878) reported no matching tested image, compiled once, passed its 16 tests and smoke test, then published normally.

### Sample CI job times

| App | Prior normal main job | Final matching main job | Final PR job |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| demo-shop | 51 s ([run 38472209797](https://github.com/samclement/demo-shop/actions/runs/38472209797)) | 35 s | 53 s |
| demo-notifier | 95 s ([run 38251176159](https://github.com/samclement/demo-notifier/actions/runs/38251176159)) | 30 s | 119 s |

These are individual jobs from start to completion, not averages or a controlled benchmark; workflow versions, caches and registry latency differ. They exclude runner queue time and Flux rollout. Retention/export work happens on the PR. The observed changed-input shop rebuild took 73 s, illustrating that matching and artifact lookup have overhead on the ordinary build path as well.

### Live verification

Image automation pinned the final shop image in `054a906` and notifier image in `368e908`; the Offers workflow recorded the shop source in `07a2e17`. Automatic console pin `ee4997b` deploys `src-b40c2c302f7cd803`. At that revision `make flux-reconcile`, `make verify-platform` and both staging `make app-status` checks passed: both reused images run with 1/1 ready replicas, the shop's TLS is Ready, and console tooling matches Git. Notification and dashboard checker jobs scheduled at 23:01 UTC on 13 October succeeded on the new image.

Signed-out shop and console requests returned HTTP 301 and HTTPS 302 with certificate verification enabled. Through a temporary Service port-forward, the shop returned 200 for `/healthz`, `/openapi.json` and `/` with a simulated user header. This does not exercise a real Google-signed-in browser session.

The configured package hooks reported successful prior deliveries but these publishes again needed signed GitHub pings to trigger the normal image scan and Git automation, as in the earlier TypeScript build change. Fixing that existing delivery gap remains tracked in the plan. Rapid bot pushes also left a cancelled GitHub Pages deployment active; it was cancelled through the Pages API and the latest publish retried. No throwaway app repositories, packages or cluster services were created for this change.
