### Staging and platform verification

Catalogue commit `37c7e7e` passed `make check` (747 tests, all 32 policy combinations), Ruff, ShellCheck and documentation validation. It also expands the canonical [build optimisation guide](apps.md#compiling-typescript-once) with adoption requirements, fallback behaviour, provenance and storage/timing trade-offs. Automatic pin `f6ef5bd` deploys the matching console/checker image `src-90c087ac99e4ba50`; both checker jobs scheduled at 04:45 UTC succeeded on it.

At platform `f6ef5bd`, `make flux-reconcile`, `make verify-platform` and all eight staging `make app-status` checks passed: each app is Healthy, running its published tested digest with 1/1 ready replicas. All five web apps have Ready TLS; Service port-forwards returned 200 for `/healthz` and each API description, and signed-out ingress requests returned HTTP 301 and verified-TLS HTTPS 302. A real Google-signed-in browser session remains unexercised by this rollout. No production pin changed and no promotion ran.

Image automation scanned these publications and committed every staging pin without manual GitHub pings. Concurrent image-pin writers caused a transient rejected push (`cannot lock ref`, `774824b` versus `64b3124`); both commits reached main, every image automation became Ready and Flux fetched the subsequent revision. The earlier intermittent package-event delivery gap remains tracked separately. No new app repository, package, service, credential or backup destination was created.

## New app creation speed (14 October 2026)

### Baseline

Submitted Kotlin web/no-database/no-API/no-events app `swhurl-try-16`, team `demo`, authenticated exposure, through the deployed console's `/new/repo` route at 05:05:07 UTC. The request used a Service port-forward and a hand-set identity header; it exercises the deployed creation job, not Google browser sign-in. The catalogue used Kotlin `v0.2.4` (`ac45c53`). [Image run 38514791717](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-try-16/actions/runs/38514791717) passed; [platform PR #66](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-platform/pull/66) auto-merged as `d05a6e8`.

| Milestone | Seconds after submission | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Image workflow observed complete | 148 | Build 72 s, smoke test 16 s, publication 5 s; dependency layer cached, app compilation and `jlink` ran |
| Platform PR observed open | 169 | Console job 13 finished at 05:07:50 UTC |
| Merge accepted | 252 | GitHub `merged_at` 05:09:20 UTC; gate's merge step took 71 s, including the full `make check` |
| Deployment created | 278 | Observed Kubernetes Deployment |
| Certificate Ready | 309 | Certificate Ready condition transition at 05:10:17 UTC |
| Deployment Available | 312 | Available condition transition at 05:10:20 UTC |
| Verified HTTPS sign-in redirect | 353 | HTTP 301 and HTTPS 302, certificate verification enabled |

Workflow and PR observations were polled roughly every five seconds; readiness rows use Kubernetes condition timestamps. The HTTPS observer initially saw Traefik's self-signed default certificate just after the Certificate became Ready, stopped, and was resumed; 353 s is the confirmed upper bound, not the instant the route first became usable. `make app-status APP=swhurl-try-16 ENV=staging` reports Healthy, 1/1 ready, matching digest and Ready TLS. `make verify-platform` passed at `d05a6e8`. No database or app Secret was created. The repository, package and staging instance remain for operator cleanup.

### Changes and validation

Kotlin [template PR #27](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-app-template-kotlin/pull/27), merged as `03872aa`, releases `v0.2.5`: an independent `java-runtime` Docker stage for `jlink`, shared registry cache tags keyed by effective Gradle kind/database/API settings, legacy cache imports, and four one-second container liveness observations instead of fixed ten-second waits. SQLite still checks migrations, writes and persistence across restart. The seven-combination [PR run 38515364232](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-app-template-kotlin/actions/runs/38515364232) and [main run 38515618757](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-app-template-kotlin/actions/runs/38515618757) passed, including retained OCI/runtime identity proof on the default combination. The main builds wrote the new public cache tags. Every workflow shell block passed `bash -n`; an extracted liveness function observed a healthy container four times and failed on the second observation of a crashed one. Failures after that observation window remain outside the smoke test.

Platform `4a87c7c` limits new-app merge-result validation to parallel `check-repo` and `check-apps`, retaining the scope, current-head validation, hold-label and base-race checks. The unit-registration comment now agrees with the frozen test fixture. `FakeRunner` tests prove new-app checks precede the push; failed validation, a moved base and shared/encrypted changes cannot push. Operator commits still require full `make check`; it passed 749 tests, all 32 staging/production policy combinations, schemas, lint and collector fixtures, and host/fixture `bash -n` passed. No protected Flux-system file was changed. [Console publish 38515985648](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-platform/actions/runs/38515985648) pinned `src-38226a17dc2ca9d6` in `9b99499`; reconciliation and platform/app verification passed, and both checker jobs scheduled at 05:21 UTC succeeded on it.

Catalogue `89a666e` pins the tested Kotlin release commit. `make check` passed again with that pin. [Publish 38516349011](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-platform/actions/runs/38516349011) pinned `src-ddcdf7bf68de1adf` in `4196932`. A verification started during the console/notify rollout reported those two units reconciling; after they settled, `make verify-platform` passed. Both checker jobs scheduled at 05:26 UTC succeeded on the new image.

### Equivalent creation after deployment

Submitted `swhurl-try-17` through the updated deployed console at 05:25:22 UTC with the baseline's same stack, features, description, team and exposure. Its Copier answers record `v0.2.5`. [Image run 38516590629](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-try-17/actions/runs/38516590629) passed; its log shows the `java-runtime` `jlink` stage `CACHED`, and app compilation/tests still ran. [Platform PR #67](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-platform/pull/67) passed app-only Validate and the narrowed merge gate, auto-merging as `9ea0250`.

| Milestone or duration | Before (`swhurl-try-16`) | After (`swhurl-try-17`) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Image build step | 72 s | 68 s |
| Smoke-test step | 16 s | 10 s |
| Complete image CI job, excluding queue | 115 s | 122 s |
| Image workflow observed complete after submission | 148 s | 159 s |
| Platform PR observed open after submission | 169 s | 178 s |
| Merge-verification step | 71 s | 21 s |
| Merge accepted after submission | 252 s | 212 s |
| Deployment Available after submission | 312 s | 247 s |
| Certificate Ready after submission | 309 s | 253 s |
| Verified HTTPS redirect observed after submission | 353 s upper bound | 260 s |

The narrowed gate removed 50 s in this pair, and app availability was about 65 s earlier. These are two individual fresh repositories, not a controlled benchmark. Runner setup, input verification, registry transfer and rollout times differ: the complete first-image workflow was slower after the change despite the cached runtime and shorter smoke test. Rollout variation also contributes to the availability difference. The baseline HTTPS observer interruption prevents attributing the full HTTPS difference to the optimisations. Prebuilt starter images or persistent runners were not implemented; compilation and CI setup remain first-image costs.

At `9ea0250`, `make verify-platform` passed, and `make app-status` for each of the 15 registered instances reported Healthy. Both apps answered signed-out HTTP with 301 and HTTPS with 302 using certificate verification. Service port-forwards returned 200 from `/healthz` and `/` with a simulated signed-in identity; `swhurl-try-17` reported the correct app name and user caller. A real Google-signed-in browser request remains unexercised. Both apps use existing telemetry, probes and digest verification; neither adds an app Secret, persistent volume, backup destination or cluster service beyond its normal app resources.

**Kept by the operator:** [swhurl-try-17](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-try-17), its GHCR package and staging instance remain as a current-template example and timing reference. Owner cleanup remains for [swhurl-try-16](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-try-16) and its GHCR package. The older baseline instance removal is recorded in [timing app cleanup](#timing-app-cleanup-14-october-2026).

### Timing app cleanup (14 October 2026)

The operator authorised cleanup, then chose to keep the updated timing app before any removal was committed or applied. `swhurl-try-17/staging`, its repository and package remain as a current-template example and timing reference. `make app-remove APP=swhurl-try-16 ENV=staging` removes only the older baseline app folder, Flux unit file and unit registration through Git. Neither timing namespace has a PVC or application Secret; their only Secrets are Helm release records and TLS certificates. No shared NATS users, Reloader configuration, real credentials or backup destination changed.

GitHub owner cleanup remains: repository `samclement/swhurl-try-16` and its same-named container package. Keep the `swhurl-try-17` repository and package. The current `gh` token has no `delete_repo` scope; both package metadata requests returned 403 requiring `read:packages`, so it cannot manage those packages either. Credentials were not expanded or borrowed from the console. Repository settings and [the owner's packages](https://github.com/samclement?tab=packages) are the deletion entry points.

For the next optimisation review, two local Copier renders from Kotlin `v0.2.5`, with distinct names and descriptions and the same web/no-database/no-API/no-events answers, were compared across the files Docker copies. Their only differing build input is `src/main/resources/application.properties`, which embeds `micronaut.application.name`. The earlier CI logs time `check shadowJar copyAgent` at 52.1 s before and 44.3 s after the runtime-stage change. Separating identity from compiled inputs could let the existing shared BuildKit cache reuse that stage; actual savings and local/deployed identity require a prototype. No optimisation code was changed in this cleanup.

Cleanup commit `f46ec79` passed `make check` (749 tests and all 32 template policy combinations), was pushed and reconciled. `make verify-platform` passed. Kubernetes lists no `swhurl-try-16-staging` namespace, no `app-swhurl-try-16-staging` Flux unit and none of its three image-automation resources. `make app-status APP=swhurl-try-17 ENV=staging` reports Healthy with 1/1 ready and Ready TLS; signed-out HTTP returns 301 and HTTPS returns 302 with certificate verification. Its Git files are restored unchanged and its repository/package must be kept.

The next proposal uses the existing cache: [Docker reuses layers whose inputs match](https://docs.docker.com/build/cache/optimize/), and [Micronaut supports configuration from environment variables and property placeholders](https://docs.micronaut.io/5.2.x/core/). The choice between runtime identity and a final packaging/configuration step needs a prototype that preserves local defaults as well as deployed naming. A single prebuilt default image remains a later option if cache downloads consume the savings; a persistent builder adds operational maintenance and is lower priority for occasional new-app creation.

## Undeployed test repository and retained data cleanup (14 October 2026)

The operator authorised deleting the undeployed GitHub repositories and their GHCR packages, and cleaning up `test-5` on the cluster. The GitHub repositories `samclement/test-1`, `test-2`, `test-3`, `test-4`, `test-5`, `swhurl-try-7`, `swhurl-try-8`, `swhurl-try-9`, `swhurl-try-14`, `swhurl-try-15` and `swhurl-try-16` are deleted; each DELETE succeeded and its subsequent repository GET returned 404. Before deletion, a live check found no Flux app unit, HelmRelease or workload for any of them. Deployed repositories `swhurl-try-10`, `swhurl-try-11`, `swhurl-try-12`, `swhurl-try-13` and `swhurl-try-17` are kept.

`make destroy-data TARGET=pvc/test-5-staging/test-5 CONFIRM=pvc/test-5-staging/test-5` passed its dry-run guards, then removed the claim and PV `pvc-d7d26dc1-d1a6-4cab-b58d-51fc5f5dce51`. The local-path provisioner logged successful deletion of its host directory at 07:08:07 UTC. The abandoned `test-5-staging` namespace, containing only its default ConfigMap, default ServiceAccount and TLS Secret after data removal, is also gone. Existing backups remain subject to normal retention. `make verify-platform` passed after the live cleanup, and `make app-status APP=<app> ENV=<env>` reported Healthy for all 14 remaining instances with 1/1 ready replicas and matching image digests.

All 11 same-named GHCR container packages are also deleted, including their 68 package versions. A separate local classic token with `read:packages` and `delete:packages` supplied package access; the fine-grained repository cleanup token cannot manage packages. Before deletion, a live check found no workload, HelmRelease or ImageRepository using those packages. Each package DELETE succeeded and its subsequent GET returned 404; the final owner package listing contains none of the 11 targets and still contains all five deployed test packages. Local cleanup credentials are SOPS-encrypted under the Git-ignored `secrets/` directory and are not deployed to the cluster.

## Needs and grants stage 3a (16 October 2026)

[Plan section 21](plan.md#21-needs-and-grants-in-swhurlyaml-then-egress--decided-15-october-2026-not-started), stage 3a: one commit per staging image.

- **Change:** `5f5105f`. An `image-dispatch` Alert on the apps' ImagePolicies sends to a `githubdispatch` Provider (`github-dispatch`), which starts the Deploy workflow (`.github/workflows/deploy.yml`, replacing the Offers workflow). `make app-deploy` reads the newest tag the policy's filter accepts from GHCR, and its digest (the sha256 of the stored manifest, which matched Flux's `latestRef` for `demo-shop:34-5dcc9c2`), then the image's offers; the run commits both. The 10 staging ImageUpdateAutomations and the setter markers are gone (`kubectl -n flux-system get imageupdateautomations`: none). Console image `src-e83d068f6a619c6c` (`57983e1`); both CronJobs scheduled on it at 04:09.
- **Token (operator's choice):** the console's `GITHUB_TOKEN`, a fine-grained token for `swhurl-platform` only (it reads the repository, gets 404 on a private one), with no expiry; `POST /dispatches` returned 204 and GitHub named `contents=write`. Copied with sops into `platform/image-automation/dispatch-secret.sops.yaml` without being printed; `make check-secrets` compares the two copies.
- **End check:** a README commit to `swhurl-try-18` (`2146eec`, 04:06:43) reached staging in one bot commit, `ef1f612` `deploy: swhurl-try-18 (deploy workflow)` (04:08:23); the pod running `4-2146eec` was Ready at 04:08:57. The image was published and the dispatch run started at 04:07:42; that push gave one dispatch run, not one per policy.
- **Timing, push to Ready:** 134 s, against 68 to 89 s (median 77 s) for five deploys the same night through image automation (`demo-shop`, `demo-shopper`, `demo-orders`, `swhurl-try-10`, `swhurl-try-11`), whose tag commit landed 0 to 3 s after the build and pod Ready about 25 s later. The new path spends 41 s from dispatch to commit (5 s queued, 1 s first job, about 8 s for the second runner, 11 s tool setup of which 4 s is sops, 18 s deploy, check and commit), and 34 s from commit to Ready. The plan expected about 25 s more than before; it is about 55 s more.
- **Faster run (`a94eda9`):** one job (the new-image check first, no second runner), sops cached, the registry read for all apps in parallel, offers not read again for an unchanged image, and `check-apps` rendering in parallel. Two more README pushes to `swhurl-try-18`: dispatch to commit 23 s (sops not yet cached; deploy step 8 s) and 18 s (cached; 4 s queued, 8 s setup, 9 s deploy step), against 41 s before. From build finished to commit: 2 s median with image automation, 35 s, then 14 s and 11 s. Push to Ready was 111 s and 112 s; `swhurl-try-18`'s build took 65 to 73 s in all three runs, against 37 to 61 s for the five apps measured before, so most of what remains against the 77 s median is the build, not the deploy.
- **Validation:** `make check` passed (766 tests, 64 template renders); Validate passed on `5f5105f`; the push-started Deploy run read every staging app and changed nothing (25 s); `make verify-platform` passed, every app `newest image …, deployed`.

- **Removed (operator's approval):** `b461519`. Flux pruned the write GitRepository `swhurl-platform-write` and its Secret `flux-image-automation-git`; `make flux-install` no longer installs image-automation-controller and deleted its Deployment, ServiceAccount and the `imageupdateautomations.image.toolkit.fluxcd.io` CRD (each `NotFound`); the five remaining controllers are Ready. The GitHub deploy key `flux-image-automation` (write) was deleted; the repository has none. Console image `src-5d91e318dea084e4` (`8be6f0d`), both CronJobs scheduled on it at 05:10; Validate passed on `b461519`; `make verify-platform` passed with no warnings.

Not exercised: a refused offer holding an image live (unit-tested), a lost dispatch caught by the hourly run, and a new app created after the change. The [repository blueprint](repository-blueprint.html) still shows image automation and the Offers workflow: rebuild it with the `repository-blueprint` skill.

## Egress stage E1 (16 October 2026)

[Plan section 21](plan.md#stage-5-egress-grants), stage 5 step E1: every app instance reaches outside through the [egress proxy](services.md#egress-proxy), which allows everything and logs each request.

- **Change:** `18fcce3` (proxy, unit `platform-egress`, ports in each instance's `HTTPS_PROXY`, rule `egress-ports`, check `egress`) and `60df6cb` (log requests only: the kubelet's TCP probes made a line every few seconds). `make check` passed. Before deploying, the generated `squid.conf` ran in `ubuntu/squid:6.6-24.04_edge` locally as uid 13 with a read-only root: a `CONNECT` on an instance's port logged that instance, the health port answered 403.
- **Rollout:** all 15 instances restarted with their port (staging 30101 to 30110, production 30111 to 30115); both proxy replicas Ready. A configuration change (`60df6cb`) rolled the two pods one at a time through the chart's checksum, without Reloader.
- **From `swhurl-try-18` (TypeScript):** Node's own `fetch` to `https://example.com/` returned 200 through its port, logged `instance=swhurl-try-18-staging … host=example.com:443 status=200/TCP_TUNNEL`. A debug container in the same pod: its own port 30110 answered 200; another instance's port (30101) and the health port timed out (NetworkPolicy); `http://notify.notify.svc.cluster.local:8080/healthz` answered 200 directly. The first check ran a second `node` process in the app container, which took the pod over its 128Mi limit: the app restarted once (lesson: check from a debug container, not `exec node`).
- **JVM apps:** `demo-notifier`, `swhurl-try-12` (both environments) and `swhurl-try-17` log `Picked up JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS` with their own port; no OTLP export errors since. `HOST_IP` is first in the rendered env, so `$(HOST_IP)` in `NO_PROXY` and `http.nonProxyHosts` expands.
- **Nothing in the cluster went through the proxy:** its log held only the test requests, no `.svc` host and no node address.
- **ClickStack:** the lines are in `otel_logs` as service `egress` (`api.github.com:443` at 16:17:08, 20 s after the request).
- `make verify-platform`: `[OK] the egress proxy runs 2 replicas`, `[OK] 15 instance(s) each have a port of their own`.

Not exercised live: a new app or first promotion getting its port (unit-tested); an app whose HTTP client ignores the variables (it still connects directly until E3).

## Faster tests and console dev loop (17 October 2026)

- **Measured before** (`e2dd2ed`): a console change took about 3 minutes from push to the new pod (Validate 91 to 144 s over 40 runs, publish about 35 s, pod 17 s after the pin commit). Locally, `make test` took 222 s and `make check` 6 min 58 s (4 CPUs shared with k3s). `test_verify.ConsoleTokenTests` alone took 217 s locally and 42 s in CI: each verifier run read the instances' HelmReleases nine times through PyYAML's pure-Python parser.
- **Changes:** `a86094e`, `make test K=<pattern>` and the slowest classes after each run (`K=test_console`: 187 tests in 8.9 s); `4cce447`, every YAML read goes through libyaml (`swhurl/yamlio.py`) and the egress check reads the instances once. Console image `src-dd6835f994000c96` deployed by `90eca68`.
- **After:** the full suite in CI took 31 s (was 59 s); locally it took 110 s inside `make check`, which took 4 min 52 s. `make verify-platform` passed with no BAD or WARN lines; `console-dashboards` and `console-notifications` succeeded on the new image.
- **check-templates** (`0a58f47`): the 64 combinations run in parallel, one process per CPU of the machine running it; prod is checked once (by the promotion conversion), and Copier renders the pinned checkout as a plain folder (0.7 s a render instead of 1.9 s). Measured apart, because the two machines differ: locally, old and new back to back took 192 s and 97 s (4 CPUs shared with k3s and ClickHouse; load 5 to 10), and `make check` took 3 min 34 s at load 16 (was 4 min 52 s); now the test suite is the slowest part of it. In CI the job took 55 s (was 88 to 112 s). The same output in every combination before and after; a failing `helm` fails every combination and exits 1 with `--jobs 4`. Console image `src-0f3a360ab47e7787` deployed by `99a24c3`; `make verify-platform` passed with no BAD or WARN lines.
- **Test CPU** (`375ad7a`): measured class by class with nothing else from the suite running, the suite used 136 CPU-seconds and now uses 111. `ConsoleTokenTests` 14 to 2.4 s and `VerifyPlatformTests` 10 to 2 s: the fake cluster read each instance's image tag with the pure-Python parser for every test, and `yamlio` now parses a given text once and hands out copies (same objects and errors as `yaml.safe_load` on all 270 YAML files in the repo). Locally the suite is CPU-bound (4 CPUs shared with k3s and ClickHouse): it took 61 to 67 s at load 8 to 17 (was 77 s at load 11), and `make check` 198 s at load 14. In CI the suite took 25 s, within the 20 to 31 s of earlier runs. Tried and reverted: one job per test instead of per class (65 to 70 s either way, alternating three runs each), and caching ruamel parses (a deep copy loses a comment above a list's first item once edited). What is left is starting processes: `CommandSafetyTests` starts Python 448 times for its fake tools (36 ms each, about 45 CPU-seconds), and `EditTests` is ruamel round-trip parsing (19 CPU-seconds). Console image `src-44b2f067fe0c7ec0` deployed by `40396d3`; `make verify-platform` passed with no BAD or WARN lines.
- **Console deploy, push to pod** (measured on `375ad7a`): 124 s (was about 3 minutes; `0a58f47` took 140 s). Validate 55 s (the tests 42 s, starting after the 6 s classification; the template check 39 s), publish 48 s (Set up Buildx 13 s, build 10 s, pin commit 6 s), pin commit to new pod 22 s (artifact 4 s after the commit; `platform-console` waits one 5 s dependency retry; Helm 5 s). `ee10b6b` checks the console's readiness every 2 s instead of 10: the new pod was ready 5 s after starting (was about 11 s, the first probe failing at 1 s); the old pod stops before the new one starts, so this is also the console's gap. `make verify-platform` passed with no BAD or WARN lines.
- **Console image built alongside the checks** (`ed7f30e`, operator's choice B): `publish-console.yml` is folded into Validate as `console-image` (on pushes only, no `needs`) and `deploy-console` (needs every other job; runs when none failed or was cancelled and the build succeeded). On `ed7f30e`: push 15:58:54 UTC, image built 15:59:01 to 15:59:22, last check (templates, 55 s this run) done 16:00:04, pin commit `a1cb527` 16:00:13, new pod started 16:00:33 and ready 16:00:39: 99 s push to pod (was 124 s). The deploy job's 9 s and Flux's 20 s are what follows the checks now. A commit whose checks fail still publishes its `src-<hash>` image; not exercised: a failing check holding the deploy (asserted on the workflow file in `test_images.PublishWorkflowTests`). `make verify-platform` passed with no BAD or WARN lines.

## Seed images and new-app auto-merge (17 October 2026)

**Change.** Templates `v0.2.12` publish the image their own CI built and smoke-tested for the default answers on each `main` commit (`ghcr.io/samclement/swhurl-app-template-<stack>:seed-<commit>`, public); a new app's first run copies the one for its template revision to `<app>:0-<sha>` in a `seed` job ([TypeScript #51](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-app-template-typescript/pull/51), #52; [Kotlin #38](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-app-template-kotlin/pull/38), #39). Platform `63b9cfe` and `7e47875`: the console and `make app-repo` take the seed or the run's own image, whichever GHCR has first, and the catalogue pins TypeScript `c268ac5` and Kotlin `0548683`. Platform `6ae2a3b`: a new app's PR auto-merges again with its generated events key (a new Secret holding its NATS seed alone, encrypted to `.sops.yaml`'s recipients) and the shared files `make app-sync` derives (the gate regenerates them on the merge result and requires no difference); since 15 October every new app had needed a manual merge for that key.

**TypeScript, `swhurl-try-19`** (default answers, through the deployed console's `/new/repo` with a port-forward and a hand-set identity header), submitted 16:45:19 UTC: seed job done 16:45:57 (copy 11 s), PR #68 open 16:46:10 (51 s) pinning `0-800692c`; the run's own `1-800692c` on GHCR about 16:46:29. PR #68 predates `6ae2a3b` and waited for the operator (merged 17:02:07); after the merge: Deployment created on the seed 25 s, Available 39 s, HTTPS 302 55 s, own image rolled out by image automation 74 s, Available on it 80 s. TypeScript's own build is fast, so the seed saved about 25 s here.

**Kotlin, `swhurl-try-20`**, submitted 17:30:19 UTC on `6ae2a3b` and `7e47875`:

| Milestone | After submission | 14 October baseline (Kotlin, auto-merged) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| First image on GHCR | 46 s (seed) | 148 s (own build) |
| Platform PR #70 open, labelled `auto-merge` | 69 s | 169 s |
| Merged by the gate, no operator step | 147 s | 252 s |
| Deployment Available (on the seed `0-758b840`) | 204 s | 312 s |
| HTTPS 302 (sign-in redirect) | 210 s | 353 s upper bound |

The app's own first build failed at Gradle: Maven Central answered "could not find" for `kotlinx-coroutines-core-jvm:1.10.2` and `symbol-processing-common-deps:2.3.12`, which exist; the template's build of the same commit had passed minutes before. A rerun of the failed job (17:43:38 to 17:45:32) passed and image automation rolled out `1-758b840` at 17:46:21. Until then staging served the seed, as documented.

**Checks.** `make check` passed before each platform commit (789 tests at the end; one `check-otel` fixture collector timed out at load 21 and passed on its own and in a full rerun). Each template's PR and `main` runs passed all 8 combinations; both seed images answer anonymous pulls with 200. `make verify-platform` passed after each console deploy; once it failed while ClickStack's MongoDB recovered from an OOM kill at its 500 MB limit (16:42 UTC, its first restart in 18 days, unrelated) and passed after it was Ready. Gate tests hold, each for its stated reason: a hand-edited shared file, an events key with another value, a plaintext value, another recipient or another app's name, and any other encrypted or shared file.

**Removed.** Both staging instances (`make app-remove`, then `make app-sync`). Their repositories and packages are left for the operator ([plan item 8](plan.md#open-work)).

**Template CI export proof restored** (TypeScript #53 `4e06690`, Kotlin #41 `cc87570`): `CHECK_EXPORT` compared the answers with the string from before the `flags` question, so the default combination had skipped the OCI export, provenance and retained-image proof. It now matches the first matrix entry exactly; on both PRs and `main` runs those steps ran and passed, and the seed (now pushed from the loaded export) was published (`seed-4e06690`, `seed-cc87570`, anonymous pulls 200). Apps are unaffected: only the templates' own CI changed.

**Not exercised:** a seed whose template revision has none (the run's own image then comes first; unit-tested only), and the gate refusing a real PR (`FakeRunner` only).

## Calls carrying the signed-in person (17 October 2026)

Stage 1 of [app types](plan.md#23-app-types-for-new-apps--decided-17-october-2026). Templates `v0.2.13` (TypeScript #54 `8b13435`, Kotlin #42 `586d545`; both PRs passed all 8 Template combinations) send `x-swhurl-user` when a call passes `onBehalfOf`, and read it only beside a valid app token. Platform commits: `2578acea` (pin, [apps](apps.md#calling-another-app)), `be68481a` (throwaway instances), `ba825842` (collector promotes logback's `mdc.user.email`).

**Live proof** with two throwaway staging apps on their own first builds, calling each other: `swhurl-try-21` (TypeScript, `1-dcba456`) and `swhurl-try-22` (Kotlin, `1-c017e3d`), both created from the `v0.2.13` seeds. Each app's `/call/<other>` was requested from inside its own pod (`kubectl debug`, curl) with `x-auth-request-email: obo-proof@example.com`, as oauth2-proxy sets it:

| Request | Callee's answer |
| --- | --- |
| TypeScript calls Kotlin for the person | `caller: {type: app, id: swhurl-try-21, user: obo-proof@example.com}` |
| Kotlin calls TypeScript for the person | `caller: {type: app, id: swhurl-try-22, user: obo-proof@example.com}` |
| TypeScript, anonymous, with a forged `x-swhurl-user` on its own request | `caller: {type: app, id: swhurl-try-21}`: no person |

ClickStack: both callees' server spans carry `user.email = obo-proof@example.com` beside `caller.type = app` and `caller.id`. The Kotlin request log first carried it as `mdc.user.email`; after `ba825842` both stacks' request logs carry `user.email` at the top level and no `mdc.user.email`. `make verify-platform` passed; `make app-status` for both instances: running, matches desired. `make check` passed before each commit (789 tests); one `check-otel` fixture run failed while a Kotlin build loaded the host and passed alone and in a full rerun.

**Not exercised:** a real signed-in browser request through oauth2-proxy (the header was set inside the pod, past Traefik); a callee on an older template (it ignores the header by construction).

**Removed** (operator approved, 17 October 2026): both staging instances (`make app-calls --remove`, `make app-remove`, `make app-sync`). Their repositories and packages are left for the operator to delete.

**Live,** both submitted at 21:47:32 UTC through the deployed console's New app form (port-forward, hand-set identity, console image `src-bb5aba36c0b90263`), team `demo`:

| App | Type | Answers in `.copier-answers.yml` | Platform PR | Available | First image |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `swhurl-try-24` | API, Kotlin | web, sqlite, openapi, asyncapi, example | #73 merged itself at 149 s | 213 s | seed `0-a612065`; own build `1-a612065` followed |
| `swhurl-try-25` | Worker, TypeScript | worker, none, none, asyncapi, example | #72 opened at 62 s; refused by the merge gate (below), merged by hand at 1211 s | 1240 s | seed `0-d953445`; `make app-deploy` moved it to `1-d953445` |

The API is private (a Service, no Ingress) with `DATABASE_PATH`; the worker has neither. Both carry `SWHURL_DESCRIPTION` from the form and publish their template's example event. `make backup-sqlite` made the API's first backup, which `make verify-platform` asks for.

**Found: two New app jobs at once conflict.** Both PRs were opened from the same `main` and edit the shared derived files (`clusters/home/kustomization.yaml`, the NATS users, the egress proxy, Reloader's namespaces), so once #73 merged, #72 conflicted and the auto-merge gate refused it. Both instances had also been given the same egress proxy port (30116), which `make check-apps` refuses. Resolved by hand: merged `main` into #72, took `main`'s derived files, added the unit, dropped `swhurl-try-25`'s proxy variables and ran `make app-sync` (port 30117), then `make check` and Validate passed. Tracked in the [plan](plan.md#23-app-types-for-new-apps--decided-17-october-2026).

**Not exercised:** the seed of a Kotlin API serving the new app's own name (its own build replaced the seed before it was read), and `prune-seeds` deleting a version (none is 30 days old yet). **Removed** (operator approved): both staging instances; their repositories and packages are left for the operator.

## Two new apps at once; removal notices (17 October 2026)

**Removal is not a failed deployment** (`c9790142`): removing an instance makes its Flux unit report `ArtifactFailed` (`kustomization path not found`, then what it prunes) for a few seconds before Flux deletes it, and the checker had sent "swhurl-try-25/staging deployment failed" for `90b5ab0c`. A unit's failure is now reported only when it is a minute old and the unit still exists. Unit-tested; not yet seen live on a removal.

**Two new apps at once** (`7bd5863f`): the auto-merge gate builds a new app's merge result on the newest `main` (`main`'s registry and derived files, the unit registered again, a taken egress port given up, `make app-sync`). Live, through the deployed console, `swhurl-try-26` (API, TypeScript) and `swhurl-try-27` (worker, TypeScript) submitted together at 22:33:57 UTC: both PRs were opened from the same `main` with egress port 30116; #74 merged itself at 156 s and #75 at 152 s, the gate giving `swhurl-try-26` port 30117; both Available on their seeds at 186 and 189 s. `make verify-platform` passed after `make backup-sqlite` made the API's first backup.

**Data destroyed** (operator approved): `make destroy-data TARGET=pvc/swhurl-try-24-staging/swhurl-try-24` deleted the claim and its volume, and the empty namespace was deleted; its backups remain in the local and S3 backup locations until retention prunes them. `swhurl-try-26` and `swhurl-try-27` were then removed the same way (operator approved), `swhurl-try-26`'s volume destroyed with `make destroy-data`.

## Code simplification design contracts (18 October 2026)

[Plan section 24](plan.md#24-code-simplification--design-contracts-18-october-2026) records the proposed operation, input, rendering, promotion, console and migration contracts. Revised twice the same day after review: primitives and use cases first, capabilities as the unit of co-location, a single cutover after a shadow period instead of per-group migration, backups moved to section 25, and automatic merge and the progress view kept but reimplemented on the primitives (one replaying gate, progress from a render hash). The operator keeps seed images and automatic merge.

This change is documentation only. The input formats, merge workflow, Activity implementation and deployed app manifests have not been refactored. `make check` passed, including 795 existing tests and both templates' policy combinations; documentation links and whitespace were checked. These checks validate the design's integration into the repository, not the future renderer or its migration. The fixture prototype, live no-op migration proof and creation timing/concurrency proof remain implementation acceptance gates.

## Backup simplification design (18 October 2026)

[Plan section 25](plan.md#25-per-database-backups--designed-18-october-2026-started-18-october-2026) defines the proposed per-database CronJobs, shared backup image and producer code, namespace ownership, scoped credentials, verified archive/metadata publication, independent freshness checks, restore proof, staged cutover and rollback. It explicitly gates cutover on the credential delivery and host-local cache decisions; existing archives, S3 destinations and restore compatibility are preserved. Section 0 points to the implementation stages and replaces the earlier tentative central-CronJob follow-up.

This is documentation only: the host timer and current MongoDB/SQLite execution paths remain in use. No backup image, CronJob, credential, storage or scheduler migration was implemented or exercised. H0 inventory/decisions and H1–H5 implementation/proof remain open. Validation of this documentation does not prove the proposed backup design; its job, failure, concurrency, network, retention and restore acceptance gates remain to be run.

`make check` passed, including 809 tests, both stack templates' combinations, app policy, schema validation, lint and collector validation. A final documentation check resolved 781 relative links; `git diff --check` passed. The existing `demo-orders/prod` instance was Healthy, with its desired image running, 1/1 replicas Ready and TLS ready at revision `ed3e946f`, before this documentation commit.

## App model: stage 1 exit and first stage 2 steps (18 October 2026)

**Stage 1 exit, throwaway `swhurl-try-29`** (an API, TypeScript, SQLite and events): created with `make app-repo` and `make app-new` (`792c174c`); the shadow reported no difference for the new app. The Deploy workflow moved staging from the seed `0-c86f48a` to its own build `1-c86f48a` (`9dba6056`), passing `make check-apps` with the shadow before it pushed. `make app-promote` created production (`25a819c2`), again with no difference; both instances ran 1/1, matching desired, and Validate passed on each of these commits. Removed the same day: `make app-remove` for both environments (`c2e18d84`), `make destroy-data` for both 1Gi claims and their volumes (throwaway data only), the two empty namespaces, and its GitHub repository and container package (confirmed absent).

**Stage 2:** `make app-sync` and every edit command's derivation render from the model (`56acd2c1`); `app-calls`, `app-egress` and `app-events` change the records and render (`0fb3544e`). `make check` passed 818 tests for each; Validate passed and deployed console image `src-f2299e9e19248e1a` (`8340e81d`). `make verify-platform` passed, a notification checker and a dashboard job succeeded on the new image, and `make app-sync` on the live tree reported nothing to rewrite.

**Stage 2, the remaining edit commands** (`63ba97ac`): `app-scale`, `app-schedule`, `app-expose`, `app-team` and `app-alerts` change the records and render. `sync.change` now refuses an instance whose files are not what its records render, listing each difference, so a hand edit is never silently dropped. `make check` passed 813 tests (five tests of in-place YAML preservation replaced by one refusal test); Validate passed and deployed console image `src-55ad1998d6920192` (`2666b670`). `make flux-reconcile` and `make verify-platform` passed; all 13 app HelmReleases are Ready; a notification checker and a dashboard job ran on the new image. `make app-scale APP=demo-shop ENV=staging ARGS="--replicas 1"` on the live tree passed the guard and wrote nothing ("already has those settings").

**Stage 2, the staging deploy** (`6760d930`): `app-deploy`, `app-offers` and `app-secret` change the records and render; a deploy is rendered (`sync.prepare`) and held on env-drift or a refused need before anything is written. `make check` passed 813 tests. Before the commit, `make app-deploy ARGS=--refresh` re-read all 8 staging images from GHCR and their `swhurl.yaml` and offers at each image's commit and changed no file. Validate passed and deployed console image `src-0ad80f09d3dca413` (`980f4957`); `make flux-reconcile` and `make verify-platform` passed; all 13 app HelmReleases are Ready; a notification checker and a dashboard job succeeded on the new image. The Deploy workflow run with `refresh=true` (run 39133316686) read all 8 again with the new code in CI and committed nothing.

**The first new image through the new deploy path** (`a35e1979`, Deploy workflow, 14:05 UTC): `swhurl-try-12/staging` moved from `45-c5dfe67` to `49-72f9fd8`; the commit changed the image, the offers commit (annotation and `offers/source.yaml`) and the new image's declared `SWHURL_API_PATH`, nothing else. Staging is Healthy on the new image.

**Stage 2, promotion** (`dbe9d403`): `make app-promote` renders production from the records; the merge gate replays a later promotion on a copy of `main` and merges only exactly that, and a person merges every first promotion. `make check` passed 805 tests (the tests of the removed conversion code went with it) and all template combinations through the new promotion. On a copy of the live tree, promoting `demo-orders` (production on build 3, staging on 34) changed only production's image pin, its offers commit and `offers/source.yaml`. Validate passed and deployed console image `src-226046e5a4f2ef9b` (`2ddbd713`); `make flux-reconcile` and `make verify-platform` passed; all 13 app HelmReleases are Ready; a notification checker and a dashboard job succeeded on the new image.

**Stage 2, new app** (`dde2868f`): `make app-new` builds the instance's records from the request and renders them once, with its NATS key made and a self-describing app's offers read first; record writing and image intake are `apps/release.py`, and `new` and `edit` no longer import each other. `make check` passed 805 tests and all template combinations (each created with the new path, then promoted and policy-checked). Validate passed and deployed console image `src-a796d3bbe819559b` (`8afa5399`); `make flux-reconcile` and `make verify-platform` passed; all 13 app HelmReleases are Ready; a notification checker and a dashboard job succeeded on the new image.

**`swhurl-try-30`'s data destroyed** (operator-approved): `make destroy-data` deleted its claim, its volume `pvc-a80bea76-…` and the host directory; its empty namespace was deleted.

**Stage 3, the cutover** (`b8cbfa9f`, 18 October 2026): every instance has `release.yaml` and `instance.yaml` and every app `app.yaml`, imported once from the manifests with each platform image's stack and resource minimum read from its `swhurl.yaml`; the commands, `make app-sync` and `make check-apps` read them, and the manifest reader is gone. The import rendered the 13 instances and both fixture trees exactly as committed. No pause was needed: each Deploy attempt resets to the newest `main` and runs `make check-apps` before it pushes, and the merge gate replays with `main`'s tooling. Validate passed and deployed the console; after `make flux-reconcile` no app pod had restarted (13 pods, all older than the push), all 13 HelmReleases are Ready and `make verify-platform` passed. A Deploy refresh (run 39141560662) then recorded each staging instance's default resources as an explicit capacity (`370d096d`, render unchanged): a rule from before the cutover in `with_needs`. Fixed (`e6ac56af`: a deploy keeps the instance's capacity) and reverted (`d590aacc`); the next refresh (run 39142301891) changed nothing. A notification checker and a dashboard job succeeded on console image `src-a262f2ab96344e8f`.

**Size** (`tools/swhurl`, from `64eaca7c` before stage 1 to `d590aacc`, stages 1 to 3): 2,050 lines added and 1,602 removed, net +448; the model is 1,178 lines (`apps/model/`, with the store and without the manifest reader). Removed entry points: the first-production conversion (`first_args`, `promotion.create`, `for_production`), the second later-promotion path (`after_promotion`, `carry_image_variables`, `carry_offers`), `derive.py`'s inference, the in-place manifest editors (`apply_needs`, `update_route`, `isolation_edits` and others), the new-app gate (`merge_new_app`, `check_new_app`, `release_taken_port`), the manifest reader and `--persistence`.

**A later promotion through the new path and the replay gate** (operator-approved, 18 October 2026): the console's code (`make console-dev` with the operator's GitHub token, dev identity) reviewed `swhurl-try-12`'s promotion from build 15 to build 49 ("Only the image changes") and opened PR #77 (`apps/swhurl-try-12/prod/helmrelease.yaml` and `offers/source.yaml`), labelled to merge itself. Validate passed and the Auto-merge gate (run 39136106300) replayed the promotion on `main` and merged it 35 s after it opened (`1cf83fd8`). Flux applied it and `swhurl-try-12/prod` is Healthy on `49-72f9fd8`.

**Stage 2, the gate's general form, proven live with `swhurl-try-30`** (`72ae3876`, operator-approved, 18 October 2026): console PR bodies carry their commands, and the gate replays a new app's `make app-new` (with the PR's events key) or a later promotion on the newest `main` and pushes the replayed tree as a merge of the PR head. `make check` passed 806 tests; Validate passed and deployed the console. The console's code (`make console-dev` with the deployed console's own tokens, dev identity) created `samclement/swhurl-try-30` (TypeScript Web app, team `demo`, signed-in) at 15:05:06 UTC and opened PR #78 on the seed `0-e5e73c0` 62 s later. The gate (run 39138955992) replayed it and merged `4385e0f8` (author `swhurl auto-merge`, parents `main` and the PR head, tree identical to the PR head as `main` had not moved) at 15:08:07; staging was Healthy at 15:08:50 (3 min 44 s from submission), and the Deploy workflow moved it to its own build `1-e5e73c0` through the new deploy path (`46f18a64`). Plain HTTP answered 301 to HTTPS and HTTPS 302 to Google sign-in. Removed the same day: `make app-remove` (`11628f5e`; Flux uninstalled the release, the namespace and its 1Gi claim are kept), and its GitHub repository and container package (confirmed absent). Its claim awaits `make destroy-data` (operator's decision).

**Not exercised live:** two new apps merging at once through the replay, a first promotion through the new path, the deployed console's promotion pages in a signed-in browser, `app-secret`, and any of the edit commands writing a change to a real instance since the change (the edit and model tests cover them on the sample instance).


## Activity from render hashes (18 October 2026)

**Stage 4 of section 24:** `e7a678d7` adds a deterministic render hash to each instance's HelmRelease metadata and derives Activity from the job, PR, current Git target and cluster state. `7de9afcb` finishes pre-hash merged jobs as **Merged (historical)** without claiming their rollout; it also corrects review of deleted encrypted files. Validate passed for both commits; the final console image is `src-d6abfc4ad132f2e4` (`6e0e4c1b`). `make check` passed 807 tests and all 120 supported template policy combinations. The offline collector fixture has a 30-second bound so parallel validation has time to flush; it still stops on failure and never retries a POST.

**Metadata-only introduction:** comparing all 13 app HelmReleases before and after the first reconcile showed the Git hash on each, unchanged generations and Ready conditions. All 13 app pods and their containers predated the annotation commit. The annotation changed no Helm specification or pod template and restarted no app.

**Real signed-in browser acceptance:** the operator completed Google sign-in in an isolated Chromium session; checks used the deployed console's pages and forms, without a supplied identity header or dev identity.

| Change | Browser evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Later promotion, job #24 / PR #79 | `swhurl-try-11` production moved to `42-4390f25`; **Live in prod**, Applied and Running done |
| New worker, job #25 / PR #81 | `swhurl-try-32` first showed Flux applying its seed `0-b0f53b1`, then **Superseded** when its own build 1 became the desired render in Git |
| Scale, job #26 / PR #82 | The worker's memory limit changed from 128Mi to 256Mi; **Live in staging**, Applied and Running done |
| Remove, job #27 / PR #83 | First **Flux is removing**, then **Removed from staging**, with the live HelmRelease and namespace absent |
| Historical job #22 / PR #76 | Under **Finished** as **Merged (historical)**; its rollout predates render hashes and cannot be tracked. The active section is empty |

A newer render replaces an unfinished change's target: **Superseded** describes that old request, not a failed app. After the later removal, the scale request is superseded too; its earlier live state was captured before removal. Removal was of a worker with `database: none`, with no volume or retained data. An initial API throwaway (`swhurl-try-31`, job #23 / PR #80) was mistakenly assumed to have no database; its SQLite declaration was caught before deployment and the PR closed unmerged. Its namespace never existed. Both throwaways' GitHub repositories and container packages were deleted with the documented cleanup tokens and confirmed absent.

**Entry points:** final browser checks returned HTTP 301 to HTTPS, then 200 with the signed-in identity. Signed-out HTTP and HTTPS both redirected to Google; neither exposed an identity. An initial check caught a transient 503 during the console rollout; the settled deployment passed.

**Live verification:** `make flux-reconcile`, `make verify-platform` and `make app-status APP=demo-orders ENV=staging` passed on `6e0e4c1b`. All 13 app HelmReleases are Ready and carry render hashes. Notification checker and dashboard jobs `29872396` succeeded on the final console image.

**Size:** stage 4's `tools/swhurl` diff from `46866761` adds 213 lines and removes 196, net +17. Removed progress machinery includes `Job.stage`, stored marks and endings, `Jobs.settle`, commit-comparison rollout checks and persisted verdicts. Immutable render targets are cached by commit; PR, current main and cluster facts are read afresh.

**Still unexercised:** two simultaneous new-app merges through the replay, a first promotion through the new path, and `app-secret`. Stage 5 (production needs, event compatibility and SQLite backups) remains planned in [section 0](plan.md#0-where-this-paused-and-what-is-left).

## Activity review fixes (18 October 2026)

**Change:** `48f1450b` replaces Activity's health adapter, which rewrote Git revisions to `render` and an Updating Flux unit to True, with an explicit rule in `apps/state.py` (`verdict(render_applied=True)`: once the render hash is proved applied, Git revisions do not count and the unit counts only when failing or suspended). Activity reads pull requests, the cluster, `main` and merged trees at once; merged pull requests are kept (a closed one can be reopened); both caches drop their oldest entry instead of clearing. A job's output is one log; `stage_of`, `sections` and `Section` are gone. Plan sections 21 and 22 name today's functions. `make check` passed 807 tests; Validate passed and deployed console image `src-213fa7ad112a2e74` (`1449ab11`).

**Live verification:** `make flux-reconcile` and `make verify-platform` passed. Notification checker and dashboard jobs `29872443` succeeded on the new image. Through a port-forward with an identity header (not a browser sign-in), `/activity` answered 200 in 1.10 s and then 1.17 s, and job #27's page in 0.64 s with one output block. There is no before measurement, so the gain from concurrent reads is not shown; the cache is in memory, so a restart still reads every merged tree once. Activity showed 5 Applied, 1 Live in prod, 3 Removed from staging, 2 Superseded, 13 Merged (historical), 1 Failed, 1 Interrupted and 1 Closed.

**Superseded, decided (operator, 18 October 2026):** `cca4e9bc` (console image `src-a12062f5a8d5dff7`, `815072f6`) follows a change to a newer release when main still renders its `instance.yaml` and `app.yaml` (a staging deploy; never a promotion), and keeps in the job record when its change was first seen at its end, so a later change shows "from <time>; a newer configuration has since replaced it". `make check` passed 809 tests and Validate passed. After `make flux-reconcile`, `make verify-platform` passed and checker and dashboard jobs `29872480` succeeded on the new image. The first Activity read stored that time on 4 of the 27 job records (the Live in prod promotion and the 3 removals); the two Superseded rows (jobs #25 and #26, whose instance was later removed) were never seen at their end and stay Superseded, now worded "replaced it before it was seen live in staging". **Unexercised live:** a new app's seed followed to its build 1, and a change shown as replaced; both are covered offline.

## Faster offline validation (18 October 2026)

**Change:** `21feb960`. `make check-templates` renders every combination but checks each distinct `swhurl.yaml` once (of 144 combinations, 24 are refused and 120 render 64 distinct files), and remembers a full pass outside the repository with a hash of what it read; `FORCE=true` renders anyway. The files it reads besides its modules were measured with an audit hook on a real run (`apps/teams.yaml`, `apps/signing-keys.json`, the platform settings and Helm repositories); a test fails if it opens another. `tests/run.py` runs each test as its own job unless its class has `setUpClass`.

**Measured on the 4-CPU host (load average 9 to 13 from the cluster itself):**

| | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `make test` alone | 165 s | 68 s |
| `make check-templates` alone, rendering | 262 s | 221 s |
| `make check-templates`, nothing it reads changed | 262 s | 0.8 s |
| `make check`, nothing the template check reads changed | 3 to 4 min | 86 to 103 s |
| `make check` with a change the template check reads | 3 to 4 min | about 280 s (both renders and tests compete for 4 CPUs) |

Per combination, Copier takes 1.3 s (0.5 s of it starting Python) and the platform half 1.3 s (Helm and kustomize, 0.6 s per environment). Validate passed on GitHub (templates 79 s, tooling tests 48 s) and deployed console image `src-21db1234284394c4` (`93528a5c`); `make flux-reconcile` and `make verify-platform` passed, and checker and dashboard jobs `29872518` succeeded on it.

## Needs that move with the image (18 October 2026)

`a08e6beb`: a staging deploy of an app with production no longer holds a new health path, uid, telemetry setting or resource minimum (`SETTINGS` rows in `per_environment.py`); a promotion copies them with the image and raises production to the image's minimum, refused when production's quota cannot hold it at its replicas. A new port, secret name or database still holds the image ([apps](apps.md#deploy-a-new-image)). Proven offline only (`test_health_path_user_telemetry_and_minimum_move_with_the_image_to_production`, `test_a_promotion_carries_a_new_port_to_production_and_its_callers`); no live instance changed, since no image declared a different need. Validate passed and deployed console image `src-4fb8ffabade58033` (`e25100a0`); `make flux-reconcile` and `make verify-platform` passed with no warning, `make app-status` says Healthy for `demo-orders` and `demo-shop` in production, and checker and dashboard jobs `29872555` succeeded on the new image.

## Backup jobs deployed suspended (18 October 2026)

Plan section 25, H0 to H2. H0 (`9d6bccfe`): IAM user `swhurl-backup-writer` may only `s3:PutObject` under `app-sqlite/` and `clickstack-mongodb/` over TLS; its key was refused listing the bucket, reading an archive and writing elsewhere. `app-sqlite/` now expires after 90 days (replaced versions after 30), like `clickstack-mongodb/`. H1 (`2f83d12a`): Validate published `ghcr.io/samclement/swhurl-backup:src-069ed3000e256548` (`sha256:f1637924…`), pullable without credentials; in a local container the SQLite job's archive decrypted to the database (integrity ok) and, without AWS credentials, it stopped at the first upload with nothing published. H2 (`096723b8`): `make flux-reconcile` created unit `platform-backup` and five CronJobs, all suspended: `backup-mongodb` (03:30 UTC) and `<app>-backup` in `demo-orders` and `swhurl-try-10`, staging and prod (03:42 to 04:13). Each app namespace's `<app>-backup` Secret and `observability/backup-writer` hold the same 40-character key as `flux-system/backup-writer` (compared by hash); no backup pod ran. `make verify-platform` passed, the four SQLite instances are Healthy, and checker and dashboard jobs `29872623` succeeded on console image `src-c3ec1292f00a3d47`. The host timer still takes the nightly backups.

## Backup jobs pilot (18 October 2026)

Plan section 25, H3 (`148e1378`): `backup-mongodb` and `demo-orders-staging`'s `demo-orders-backup` are unsuspended; the other three SQLite jobs stay suspended and the host timer still runs. One Job created from each CronJob (`backup-pilot-1`) succeeded on its first run and published `sqlite-20261018T210930Z` and `clickstack-mongodb-20261018T210930Z`, archive then metadata. Restored from S3 with the private key: the SQLite archive and its plaintext match their metadata's SHA-256, `integrity_check` is ok and `orders` holds 1,000 rows; `make live-test-restore-mongodb` with the job's archive restored it into `recovery-test` with collection counts matching (14 collections). The SQLite upload went through the egress proxy (`instance=demo-orders-staging granted=- … host=swhurl-platform-backups-110927251694.s3.eu-west-2.amazonaws.com:443 status=200`), and both jobs' lines are in ClickStack's `otel_logs` (service name is the Job's name).

## Backup jobs beside the timer (18 October 2026)

Plan section 25, H4 part one (`3d193d8c`): all five backup CronJobs are unsuspended, so from 19 October each database is backed up by its job and by the host timer. `make verify-platform` now judges freshness from S3 alone, counting a backup only with its metadata, and expects one for every instance whose `release.yaml` declares SQLite; it passed with MongoDB 0.3 h old and the four SQLite databases at most 13.9 h old. The cutover (`make backup-*` start Jobs from the CronJobs; the timer retired) is on local branch `h4-cutover`, to merge once the operator has switched the timer off.

## Backup jobs cutover (19 October 2026)

The operator then ran `make host-backup-delete`; `swhurl-backup-mongodb.timer` no longer exists. Cutover `3883dbef` (`make check` passed, 827 tests): `make backup-sqlite DRY_RUN=true` listed one Job per CronJob, including the new `swhurl-try-28-staging`; `make backup-sqlite` then ran all five, each published its archive and metadata (2 or 3 tables, integrity ok) and exited 0. `make verify-platform` passed with every SQLite backup 0.0 h old and MongoDB 2.8 h old. `make backup-mongodb` ran its Job the same way (14 collections, published). Not exercised: a missed-run retry.

Renovate's updates for `demo-shop`, `swhurl-try-11` and `swhurl-try-12` failed on the template's validator, as expected, and were closed: `demo-shop` moves by hand in F5, the other two stay on their template versions.

## One rule for declarations and protected Streams (19 October 2026)

[Plan section 27](plan.md#27-one-rule-for-what-swhurlyaml-declares--decided-19-october-2026-done-19-october-2026), S1–S6. **Deployed:** S1–S5 `951303aa`, S6 `c362b5d4`, console image `src-6d08295744e16f68` pinned by `fb14e595`. [Validate on S6](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-platform/actions/runs/39234126228) passed. The console and notify run that image; both checker CronJobs succeeded on it at 10:01 UTC and continued scheduling. Independent re-review cleared the AsyncAPI parser fixes with no remaining blockers. Pre-commit `make check` passed (816 tests, lint, policy and render checks) and `make check-templates` passed.

**Live protection (10:05 UTC):** all nine app Stream resources have `spec.preventDelete: true`, Ready=True, and `status.observedGeneration` equal to their current generation. These are demo-orders, demo-shop, swhurl-try-10 and swhurl-try-12 in staging and production, plus swhurl-try-13 in staging. `make verify-platform` found all 12 declared Streams and consumers in NATS. No events, Stream resources, retained messages, credentials or namespaces were removed. This proves protection is configured and observed, not a destructive deletion test; retained broker Stream cleanup still needs explicit operator approval ([lifecycle](apps.md#events)).

**API metadata:** `make app-offers APP=demo-shop ENV=staging` and `ENV=prod` read the selected image's declaration at `a244f82`. Commit `0a4fddfa` clears only the stale `apiPath`, the HelmRelease's API-description annotation and its render fingerprint in both environments; the `visited` publication, flags and image are unchanged. `make check` passed again (816 tests), then the commit was pushed and `make flux-reconcile` completed. All ten web instances have rolled out the removal of `SWHURL_API_PATH`; it is absent from every app pod. Their current ReplicaSets were created between 09:59 and 10:01 UTC, and each current app container is Ready with zero restarts. The metadata-only offers refresh did not create another demo-shop ReplicaSet.

**Health:** at `0a4fddfa`, `make verify-platform` passed with no BAD or WARN lines. `make app-status APP=<app> ENV=<env>` for every one of the 13 instances reported Healthy, that revision applied, 1/1 replicas Ready and the running digest matching Git. TLS is Ready for every routed instance.

**Entry points:** with normal certificate verification and no cookies, HTTP returned 301 to HTTPS and HTTPS returned 302 to Google sign-in for demo-shop staging and production (`/`, `/api/shop`, `/api/buy`, GET requests only), demo-orders staging and production (`/`, `/openapi.json`), and the console (`/`, both demo-shop instance pages, `/new`). Through a port-forward to the deployed console with a hand-set `X-Auth-Request-Email`, all four demo-shop/demo-orders instance pages and `/new` returned 200. Demo-shop has no API-description row in either environment; demo-orders retains its `/openapi.json` link. The pages offer no publishing inputs or publication-removal forms, and direct developers to `asyncapi.yaml`. The port-forward was stopped afterwards.

**Not exercised:** a real Google-signed-in browser session after this rollout; removing a final live publication or deleting a protected Stream resource (deliberately not tested); a new app created on this console image. Failure-without-writes, final-publication removal, subscriber guards, subscription independence, idempotency and obsolete-input refusals are covered offline. `todo.txt` was untouched.

## DRY_RUN=true is refused where a command has no dry run (19 October 2026)

Found during the [plan 28 R3 live check](#app-new-reads-swhurlyaml-once-at-the-images-commit-19-october-2026): `make app-remove … DRY_RUN=true` removed the instance's files, because commands without a dry run (every Git edit command, and the read-only ones) ignored `DRY_RUN`. **Deployed:** `9488ead8`; console image `src-494d6203146feeb5` pinned by `c4589323`. `swhurl make` now refuses `DRY_RUN=true` for a target with no † mark (exit 2, nothing done); targets with a dry run are unchanged. `make app-remove APP=swhurl-try-10 ENV=staging DRY_RUN=true` printed the refusal and left the working tree clean. `make test` and `make check` passed (819 tests); Validate passed and deployed the console. After `make flux-reconcile`, `make verify-platform` passed with no BAD or WARN lines, and checker and dashboard jobs `29873519` succeeded on the new image. Not changed: Makefile recipes that do not run through `swhurl` (`flux-reconcile`, `flux-bootstrap`, `check`, `test`) still ignore `DRY_RUN`.

## A rollback promotion is merged by a person (19 October 2026)

From the [gate review](plan.md#gate-review-19-october-2026). **Deployed:** `66ad33ff`; console image `src-bf5a6106a61b73a6` pinned by `72fa5ee7`. A promotion whose staging image is an older `<run>-<sha>` build than production's opens without `auto-merge`, says why in its merge plan, records `rollback` in its review metadata, and cannot be resumed; the merge gate works the rollback out from the reviewed image and production's release and holds it, whatever the PR body says. The gate test that claimed a newer staging build keeps the reviewed image edited only `helmrelease.yaml`; it now makes a real deploy (`release.yaml`) and shows that such a build holds an open promotion, as `console.md` and `apps.md` now say. `make check` passed (822 tests) and [Validate](https://github.com/samclement/swhurl-platform/actions/runs/39270516184) passed and deployed the console. After `make flux-reconcile`, `make verify-platform` passed with no BAD or WARN lines; the notification checker and dashboard jobs `29873679` succeeded on the new image; `make app-status` showed `demo-shop` and `demo-orders` Healthy in both environments at `72fa5ee7`.

**Not exercised:** a real rollback promotion from the console, or the gate holding one live; the offline tests cover both.
